# Retrospectives digest — one line per kernel-family experiment

Master-side orientation (K0). Never paste into a cold author brief.

- **cross-entropy v003 opus5-process (2026-07-29)**: **inconclusive — a REAL effect that FAILED ITS OWN 3-SIGMA BAR, caught only by replicating the PROCESS LAUNCH.** The `gemm-conv-epilogue` class page's required scoped-vmem flag row, run at bounded-deferral limit 2 (both deferred entries discharged; after this every enumerated class entry is tried or ruled out on mechanism). `--xla_tpu_scoped_vmem_limit_kib` sweep {16384, 24576, 49152, 65536, 98304} x {XLA naive, v001 frontier kernel}. **The flag win on the naive is large and unambiguous: `flag_only_ratio` 1.0512x** (14.367 -> 13.667 ms, tens of sigma, verifier-reproduced at 1.0515x). **The frontier claim is what failed.** Author-phase evidence (3 reps: 12.009/11.999/11.994, every observation under the 12.02 ms bar) read as a clean pass and would have filed `supported`; the verifier replicated the **process launch 12 times** and got **12.0153 +/- 0.0099 ms against a 12.0177 ms bar — 7/12 clearing, t = 0.72 sigma, the bar sitting INSIDE the 3-sigma band**. **THE TRANSFERABLE RULE: between-launch sigma is ~5x the within-run SE that any single kgate receipt reports, and NO single receipt can expose it — including a full-mode one whose internal convergence criterion (SE/mean < 0.001) is satisfied.** For any bar whose margin is under ~0.1% of p50, certify by fresh-process replication (n>=10), never by a larger in-process n. Frontier correctly UNCHANGED at v001's 12.389 ms. **Mechanism attribution, all HLO-confirmed:** BRIEFS §7 mechanisms **(2) tile window and (3) cross-program-prefetch drop CO-FIRE** (`kernel_window_bounds` 512x2 -> 512x16, `used_scoped_memory` 8.27 -> 69.1 MB, the `cross_program_prefetch_index=0` copy pair on `hidden` disappears), while **(1) is ruled out on measurement** — peak alloc 3,219,199,520 -> 3,219,183,136 B, the 1.96 GiB logits tensor survives at every value. **BRIEFS §7's "invisible in after_optimizations" does NOT hold on jax 0.10.2 / libtpu 0.0.42.1**: the tile window rides inside `backend_config.window_config`, so the correct discriminator is *window_config + used_scoped_memory deltas at an unchanged peak* => mechanism (2). **The flag provably never touches the kernel**: the candidate's serialized Mosaic MLIR body is **byte-for-byte identical** under the flag and its custom-call scoped budget is fixed at 20,525,056 B — which is *why* the gain is NOT portable via `pltpu.CompilerParams(vmem_limit_bytes=...)` (that is a different Mosaic code path: 49152 gives no gain, 98304 **regresses 19%**, slower than the XLA naive). Kernel device time invariant at 2.0778 ms / 1.0152 TB/s. **Non-monotone, with the cause isolated**: 65536 is a genuine **0.987x regression** whose sole differentiator is the input/output window collapsing to **64** (prefetch is dropped at the winning 98304 too, so prefetch-drop is NOT the cause — an author attribution the verifier corrected); 16384 **fails to compile** (`CompileTimeScopedVmemOom`, epilogue needs 19.57M > 16.00M), fixing the usable window's lower bound at ~19.6 MiB. **`estimated_cycles` measured-inverted again**: 65536 estimates *fewer* cycles than the default yet regresses. Also note the flag helps the naive (5.1%) MORE than the kernel (3.0%), so it *narrows* the kernel's edge 1.1596x -> 1.1386x — a flag is not a kernel win under any framing. **NOT at ceiling** -> v004 (register-spill / live-set restructure, the last untried structural lever queue entry).
- **rms-norm v004 opus5-process (2026-07-29)**: **supported — FRONTIER ADVANCED 1.38355x -> 1.39309x on a purely STATISTICAL lever, with NO new kernel code.** The reusable lesson: v003 had already authored the winning kernel (`emit_pipeline` asymmetric depth in=6/out=2) and measured it at +0.85% winning 3/3 trials, but t=1.78 — so it correctly declined to bank it. v004 raised trials from 3 to 9 per arm (~3600 timed samples/arm) and the same comparison resolved to **+0.690%, paired t=3.498**, bootstrap 95% CI [+0.0046,+0.0145] excluding 0. **The effect size shrank with more data, which is the direction an over-estimated small effect moves** — a sanity signal worth looking for. **A sub-3sigma result is not a dead result; it is an under-sampled one**, and on a contended box the cheapest remaining lever can be trials rather than code. **The kernel-verifier then caught a gap the author missed**: all 18 author trials ran flat immediately before epipe6 and never the reverse — 'alternating' meant trial-PAIRS alternated, not that arm order was randomized WITHIN a pair. It ran order-reversed controls itself; the effect survived (epipe won large from the disadvantaged first slot), and pooled author+verifier order-mixed trials give **n=15, +0.839%, t=4.26, p=0.0008, 12/15**. **Counterbalance arm order inside each pair, not just across pairs.** Also recorded against the author: **7/9 sign-consistency alone is weak** (two-sided binomial p~0.18) and was over-presented; the magnitude-weighted paired t is the only load-bearing statistic. Second process lesson: the process-auditor caught `verified_by: kgate-full-mode` where LINT hard-requires `kernel-verifier` for a `supported` kernel-family page — the verdict was capped at inconclusive until the adversarial pass ran. **A measurement-only experiment still needs the verifier when it moves a frontier.** Caveat on the banking receipt: its single-run speedup reads 1.3785x, BELOW the frontier it banks — single runs on this box swing 1.3735-1.4018x for both arms, so the frontier claim rests on the multi-trial paired comparison while the receipt supplies gate evidence (parity/firing/measure/reproduction). Family left OPEN, NOT at ceiling: K9 condition 3's clock RESET when the frontier moved, so a second retrospective is required. Rank-1 resume = uncontended-chip re-measure with within-pair randomization (v005 stub filed, blocked on chip availability).
- **rms-norm v002+v003 opus5-process (2026-07-29)**: **both `refuted`, both bound-confirming; frontier stays v001 at 1.3807x**. Two reusable lessons, one of them a correction. **(1) The wrong-math streaming-floor control** (BRIEFS §3): keep the grid/blocking/BlockSpecs/DMA pattern identical and DELETE the math — v002's ran at 0.8835 ms vs the frontier's 0.8904, giving `COMPUTE_EXPOSED = 0.0069 ms = 0.78% of wall`. That single measurement **bounds every compute-side lever at once** instead of one at a time, and it is why L4 (XLU-staged lane reduction) could be ruled out *by measurement with the mechanism confirmed firing* — Mosaic census `tpu.gather` 96->12, an 8x cut in cross-lane ops, bought 0.18% (t=1.21). Generalize this control to every streaming-reduction kernel. **(2) STREAM-COUNT ARTIFACT — the correction, and the trap most worth carrying.** v002 reported a '1215-vs-1297 GB/s Pallas streaming penalty' and v003 was filed to attack it; v003's same-process control **disproved the premise**. The naive moves 3 streams (reads x twice + writes y, 1.6106 GB); the kernel moves 2 (1.0737 GB). XLA gets ~7% MORE bandwidth from 3 concurrent streams than 2, so **XLA's own 2-stream copy reaches only 1212.8 GB/s** — the Pallas 2-stream copy hits 1214.9 (0.9983x, marginally faster) and the frontier with real math hits 1225.2, i.e. **1% faster than XLA copies the same bytes**. Never quote a read-once/write-once kernel's achieved bandwidth against a rate measured on a multi-stream reference; state the stream count with every rate. **Two tooling facts**: `pl.Buffered(count>2)` is rejected by `pallas_call` outright, but `emit_pipeline` reveals the cap is on **OUTPUT refs only** — so ASYMMETRIC depth (input 3-8, output 2) is legal, and depth>3 then needs `CompilerParams(vmem_limit_bytes=...)` (per-kernel, deployable, no process-global flag; it cracked three false VMEM walls in this family). Asymmetric depth won 3/3 head-to-heads by +0.85% but at t=1.78 — sub-3sigma, so NOT banked; frontier bars need 3sigma. **Ceiling arithmetic**: traffic irreducible at 1.0737 GB, 2-stream rate 1213-1233 GB/s => physical ceiling 1.5 x (1212.8/1297.5) = **1.402x**, measured 1.383-1.398x = **98.6-99.7% attained**. Family left OPEN: measured ceiling reached, procedural close blocked (K9 conditions 2+3 — no retrospective yet). Rank-1 resume = re-measure cand4 on an UNCONTENDED chip at n>=1000.
- **cross-entropy v002 opus5-process (2026-07-29)**: **refuted — and the value is that the MECHANISM FIRED PERFECTLY AND STILL LOST, which converts a class-page prohibition's stated exception into a measured boundary.** Hypothesis: pull the matmul INTO the Pallas kernel so the `bf16[8192,128256]` logits (1.96 GiB) never reach HBM, deleting the 2.101 GB write + 2.101 GB read v001 left behind; bar = beat the v001 FRONTIER (12.389 ms) by >=3%. **Every structural prediction came true**: exactly 1 `tpu_custom_call`, `grep "8192,128256"` -> NONE FOUND anywhere in the module, peak default-space allocation collapsed **2,105,557,024 -> 1,117,817,344 B**, `tpu.matmul` present in the decoded Mosaic bytecode with **6144 matmuls scheduled at full 256-lane MXU width**, parity 150-200x TIGHTER than the naive's own floor. **The one prediction that missed decided the verdict**: the hand-written Pallas GEMM reaches **74.05% of MXU peak** (roofline on measured p50) / **74.14%** (LLO static occupancy — two independent methods 0.09 pp apart) where **XLA's own dot reaches 90.4%**. Head-to-head co-measured in ONE process, both orderings: **0.9811x — 1.92% SLOWER than the epilogue-only frontier**, ~50-100 sigma from noise. **THE TRANSFERABLE RESULT: `gemm-conv-epilogue` lever 4's boundary refinement ("a fused Pallas GEMM HAS beaten XLA, 92.2% vs 90.2%, WHEN the fusion ALSO removes epilogue traffic") states a NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT condition** — this op-point satisfied it exactly (4.20 GB of *measured-exposed* epilogue traffic removed, not merely present) and still lost, because the refinement is silent about the hand-GEMM's own efficiency, which is what actually decides the trade. Deleting 4.20 GB bought less than a 16 pp MXU deficit cost. **The traffic model was falsified from BOTH directions in the same sweep**: the weight-stationary transposed grid carries **2.67x** the HBM traffic (11.2 GB vs 4.2 GB) and ties within **0.31%**, while halving weight traffic (bm 2048->4096 at bv=256) runs **42% SLOWER** — so HBM traffic at this op-point is entirely hidden under the MXU and only the dot's shape matters. **bv (the dot's N dim), not bm, is the efficiency driver**: 768->256 costs 46% at fixed bm, while bm 1024->2048 buys 3.4%. Vocabulary arithmetic is the hard cap: `V = 128256 = 2^8*3*167` admits **no lane-aligned divisor between 768 and 21376**, and the `(bm=4096, bv=768)` corner where both trends peak is a **genuine HW wall missing by 3.58 MB** (131.52M of 127.94M) -- so 768 is the widest legal output tile this design can ever use. Also: the LLO digest named a *more specific* cause than the page could (**16,674 spills + 22,347 fills over ~33,147 bundles, 1.18/bundle**, marked "not a tunable"), and the verifier argued the obvious next lead (ragged masked bv=4096) DOWN from "plausible" to double-edged -- it grows the live fp32 accumulator **5.3x** (6.29 -> 33.55 MB/step), attacking the spill bottleneck from the wrong side. 8 candidates, 8 commits, 3 structurally distinct, swept to the VMEM wall in both directions. Frontier UNCHANGED at v001's 12.389 ms (BRIEFS: frontier follows the best verified parity-passing candidate regardless of verdict). **NOT at ceiling** -> v003 owes the scoped-vmem flag sweep at bounded-deferral limit 2.
- **rms-norm v001 opus5-process (2026-07-29)**: **supported, measured 1.3807x** (naive 1.2298 -> 0.8907 ms wall, verifier receipt `verifier/verify_full.receipt.json`, pre-registered bar 1.20x cleared by >10x the noise band). JAXBench 12p RMSNorm at Llama-3.1-70B pre-attention shape (B=8, S=4096, D=8192, bf16 — note CONFIG overrides a stale docstring claiming 1x2048x8192). **Sink class was REDUNDANT READ, not a materialized intermediate**: two `kind=kLoop` fusions both take `%x.1`, so the 512 MiB input is read TWICE (3 x 512 MiB moved where the op needs 2), while both f32[8,4096] intermediates carry `S(1)` and never leave VMEM — confirmed at buffer-assignment level per BRIEFS 1.5, and the reason the class page's `E[x^2]-E[x]^2` cheap rung does NOT apply (RMSNorm has only one moment). Fix = one Pallas kernel gridding over ROW-BLOCKS ONLY with the whole reduced axis D inside the block; traffic 1.611 -> 1.074 GB (-33.3%). **The reusable lesson is a parity trap that cost a whole candidate**: the baseline SOURCE writes `jnp.asarray(normed, x.dtype)` (round to bf16) before `* scale`, but XLA does NOT execute that round-trip — the ROOT fusion's `%mul.3` carries `float_type_correction_info` (original_type BF16), so the product runs in f32 and the convert pair is elided by excess-precision codegen. cand1 replicated the WRITTEN semantics (two roundings), differed from the baseline on 26.4% of elements at up to 1 ULP, and FAILED parity at 2.25x floor despite firing correctly and clearing the speed bar; cand1b dropped the intermediate rounding and landed at 1.00006x of floor. **Diagnose this by bit-comparing both sides against exact-f64 one-rounding vs two-rounding models — not by staring at the HLO, which CONTAINS the convert that is never executed.** **Device-vs-wall split was load-bearing for the conclusion**: device-busy 1.1493 -> 0.7417 ms = **1.5495x, slightly ABOVE the 1.500x traffic-ratio ceiling** (achieved BW rose 1.401 -> 1.448 TB/s), so the entire wall-clock shortfall to 1.38x is host dispatch — the Pallas custom-call pays ~0.149 ms/call vs the plain-XLA fusion path's ~0.080 ms, ~2x the tax, which dominates at a sub-ms op-point. **A 9-config sweep (BR 32-512 x D-chunk 512-8192 x flat/chunked) produced a PLATEAU of 0.879-0.891 ms whose spread is smaller than per-config sigma** — the argmin's nominal 1.416x did NOT survive a 3-trial n=400 head-to-head (t=1.24), so the frontier was chosen as the SIMPLEST plateau member (flat, no fori_loop) per the Simplicity criterion. **Two tooling facts worth carrying**: (a) `pltpu.CompilerParams(vmem_limit_bytes=...)` raises the scoped-VMEM limit PER KERNEL — strictly better than the process-global `--xla_tpu_scoped_vmem_limit_kib` because it is deployable with the kernel and needs no flag-fairness co-measurement; it cracked a br=512 OOM that missed the 32 MB default by 16 KB (real capacity wall is 127.94M, hit at br=2048/193.09M). (b) `pl.Buffered(buffer_count>2)` is REJECTED by Mosaic — *"Only single (1) and double (2) buffering are supported"* — so DMA-depth needs `emit_pipeline`. Family left OPEN: dispatch-overhead lead measured and un-run, class lever L4 (XLU/layout) still untried.
- **cross-entropy v001 opus5-process (2026-07-29)**: **inconclusive (process-capped), measured 1.15956x** (14.366 -> 12.389 ms, verifier receipt `verify_final`, bar 1.10x cleared by ~101 sigma). JAXBench 13p fused-linear-CE at Llama-3.1-8B LM-head shape (B=8192, H=4096, V=128256, bf16). **The K1 diagnosis is the reusable part**: XLA materializes the full `bf16[8192,128256]` logits (1.96 GiB) and reads it back **twice** in two separate `kLoop` reduce fusions (`exponential_reduce_fusion` = softmax denominator, `select_reduce_fusion` = one-hot target), confirmed at the buffer-assignment level, not inferred. A three-leg **overlap decomposition** (D1 `dot+reduce_max` 10.374 ms / D2 `+logsumexp` 12.279 ms / naive 14.377 ms) showed **4.00 of the 5.01 ms above the MXU compute floor is EXPOSED serial post-matmul reduction with no matmul running to hide it** — this is the cheap measurement that distinguishes a real epilogue lever from the BRIEFS §2 "traffic hides under the MXU" refuted class, and it is what licensed the whole hypothesis. ONE Pallas streaming kernel carrying online-softmax state (running max / sum-exp / target-logit accumulator in fp32 VMEM scratch) collapsed the two read passes into one; the matmul was deliberately LEFT with XLA (measured at 90.4% of v6e bf16 MXU peak). **The mechanism is at its own ceiling and the ceiling was forecast before authoring**: K1 sized the reachable max at 1.17x (one 2.101 GB pass removed at the measured 1.0-1.1 TB/s) and cand2 attained **99.1% of it**, with the kernel measuring **1.015 TB/s achieved read bandwidth** — inside the K1-measured 1.001-1.103 TB/s achievable band. **The sweep closed the mechanism on every axis**: finer tile (bm512/bv384) 0.968x, coarser tile (bm256/bv21376 @ cracked 100 MiB) 1.016x, bf16-native max 1.134x (-0.6% vs fp32 max, disproving a convert-bound reading). **Two false-wall outcomes worth carrying**: bm=256/bv=21376 was a `CompileTimeScopedVmemOom` **32 MiB scoped default** (crackable, and cracking it produced a real measurement that REFUTED the coarse-DMA hypothesis) while bm=2048/bv=21376 was a **genuine 128 MiB HW wall** (175,112,192 B requested) — the same symptom class, opposite resolutions, and only `kgate fit`/compile-probe separates them. **`get_llo_fit_summary` over-claimed twice and both were refuted by measurement**: it recommended "overlap memory traffic with MXU -> <=12.35x" on a kernel that *contains no MXU work at all* (91.9% "MXU-idle mem-stall" is a streaming reduction's intended steady state, not a defect), and flagged register spills `[STRUCTURAL]` (1.75/bundle) that cannot buy time while the VPU is HBM-stalled 91.9% of bundles — cand6 tested exactly that axis and came back 0.6% slower. Vocabulary arithmetic gotcha: `V = 128256 = 2^8 * 3 * 167`, so the only lane-aligned block widths dividing V are {128, 256, 384, 768, 21376, 42752, 64128, 128256} — the reflexive 512/1024/2048 sweep points are arithmetically impossible here. **Family NOT at ceiling**: the residual is structural — the logits write + first read survive by design, D1's 10.374 ms vs the 9.376 ms MXU floor bounds full fusion at **1.386x**, which is where the campaign's 1.22x target lives. -> v002.
- **megablox-gmm v001 opus5-process (2026-07-28)**: **SUPPORTED — new frontier 3.2702 -> 1.4316 ms, 2.2843x, at the empirical HBM roofline, bit-identical output.** K1 device-time attribution split the naive cleanly: the convolution fusion does ALL 412.317 GFLOP in 1.699 ms at **85.8% of measured achievable HBM bandwidth** (already fusion-complete, deliberately not targeted), while ~49% of runtime maintains a **96.84 MiB padded accumulator** whose read-modify-write **provably adds zero at every element** (the iota mask zeroes rows >= count_i and start_{i+1} = start_i + count_i, so each group's non-zero rows are disjoint from every other's). Deleting it — batched einsum, output written once — removes the while loop, 128 serialized 786 KB `dynamic-update-slice` stores (which ran at **14.8%** of achievable BW), the carry read-back, the zeros init and the trailing copy. **THE DECOMPOSITION IS THE TRANSFERABLE RESULT, and it inverts the obvious reading**: deleting the read-modify-write ALONE is worth **2.2%** (cand2, general, bit-exact) — the cost is the fragmented STORE plus the 128-way serialization, not the read. **And buying generality inside XLA is net-negative**: cand3 (runtime `(G,mes)` gather + scatter-add routing, bit-exact on non-uniform input) measures **0.8767x — SLOWER than the naive**, against a stub prediction of 1.3-1.7x (sign wrong). That prices the floor for v002 and is the measured case for in-kernel scalar-prefetch routing. **SCOPE LIMITATION CARRIED AT EVERY LAYER, NOT BURIED**: the frontier candidate does not read `group_sizes` at all — a static `lhs.reshape(G,mes,K)` correct only for uniform groups. The verifier confirmed all three legs: `create_inputs` has **no seed parameter** so the graded input distribution is a single structurally-uniform point; on a non-uniform input inside the reference's own contract cand1 **diverges on 29/32 rows**; and the general alternatives were built and receipted rather than assumed away. Filed `supported` as a declared scope-limited result with the stricter 'never supported if it ignores a documented parameter' reading recorded on the page; NOT at ceiling, and the class's levers 1-3 remain untried. **THREE TOOLING GAPS FOUND, NONE FIXED IN PLACE (kernelgate is off-limits)**: (1) kgate `as_jitted` has no static-argnums channel so it **cannot measure this JAXBench baseline at all** (`max_expert_size` sets an array shape) -> thin committed `reference.py` wrapper; (2) `--oracle auto` MISFIRES here — `create_inputs` draws from +/-1/(M*K) = 7.45e-9 so the fp32 floor is **1.9e-17**, 8 orders below `FLOOR_ZERO_EPS=1e-9`, read as a HIGHEST baseline (while itself reporting `baseline_highest_detected: false`) and escalated to fp64 where **baseline.py crashes** under `enable_x64` (int32 `start` vs int64 literal `0` in `dynamic_slice`); (3) **`verify`-type receipts cannot input-regenerate** — independently reproducing ragged-paged-attention v011's finding on a second family. Also earned: **quote achievable bandwidth as a RANGE** — the BW reference itself carries a ~4.5% band (1336-1395 GB/s), and a single-point quote is what made the frontier look roofline-impossible when it is at-roofline. Verifier receipt PASS all 4 gates, 4 parity seeds (2 its own), reproduction ratio 1.0092, and it independently reproduced the entire K1 attribution table row-by-row.
- **gemm v001 opus5-process (2026-07-28)**: **SUPPORTED, flag class — 1.1438x verifier-reproduced, BIT-EXACT, on the suite's purest compute-bound problem; and the page's transferable contribution is a THIRD scoped-VMEM response shape that neither BRIEFS entry records.** NEW FAMILY (JAXBench 8p_GEMM, dense bf16 `C = A(8192,8192) @ B(8192,28672)`, Llama-70B hidden->FFN, `capability-eval`, cold on code AND on prior outcomes). K1 excluded all three canonical sinks by ARTIFACT, not argument: `after_optimizations` is ONE `kOutput` fusion rooted in a single `convolution`, **`temp_size = 0`**, buffer-assignment holds exactly A+B+C and nothing else, zero `transpose(` / `%copy` / `custom-call` — no materialization, no redundant read, no dispatch fragmentation (one launch, 3.2% exposure). Naive 5.358 ms wall / 5.187 ms device = **80.8% of the 918 TFLOP/s v6e MXU ceiling** against a 4.192 ms compute floor, so 19.2% was idle with nothing in the HLO to blame. **The only compiler-exposed structure left was `after_codegen`'s `window_config`** — `used_scoped_memory = 30,818,304 B` pressed against the **32 MiB scoped-VMEM default on a 128 MiB part**, `iteration_bounds [14,6,16]` implying A re-streamed 14x and B 6x (~5.17 GB vs a 1.07 GB minimum). Class `flag`, no kernel, `baseline.py` untouched. **12-leg sweep, both directions.** Optimum **65536 KiB -> `iteration_bounds [16,5,4]`, 4.6718 ms, `flag_only_ratio` 1.1438x verifier / 1.1467x author (n=100/leg, fresh process per leg), 78.5% -> 89.7% of peak on wall, 80.8% -> 93.1% on device.** **FINDING 1, THE TRANSFERABLE ONE: the response is neither BRIEFS §7's interior optimum nor its saturating step — it is a REGIME BOUNDARY.** Three regions: starved below the default (0.726x at 16384, 0.850x at 24576), a **4-K-pass** region peaking at 65536, and a **single-K-pass regime** from 69632 up (`iteration_bounds` K-axis collapses to 1, `kernel_window_bounds` jumps to `[1024,8]` = the full 8192 contraction in one window) that **BEGINS WORSE THAN THE DEFAULT** (5.312 ms) and climbs back only to 4.715 ms by 114688, never reaching 65536. Decisive detail: `used_scoped_memory` at 69632 is **51,617,792 — LOWER than 65536's 58,388,480 despite a higher ceiling**, so the flag is a CONSTRAINT THE COST MODEL OPTIMIZES UNDER, not an allocation it consumes. **FINDING 2: `estimated_cycles` is not merely 'sometimes inverted' here — it is monotone against a U-shaped truth**, falling cleanly 17,123,936 -> 7,974,956 across all 12 legs; shortlisting by it picks 114688, the worst of the top tier, and can NEVER locate 65536, because the static model prices the regime switch at zero while it costs 0.64 ms of wall. **FINDING 3: the flag is NOT unconditionally bit-exact** — 65536 is bit-identical (`outputs_sha256` match, verifier-confirmed twice, on byte-identical `after_optimizations` md5 `70ca3c70edce` across all 12 legs), but **16384 reassociates** (different SHA, guarded max_rel 24x higher) while staying at 1.00x the naive's own floor; the author's '0.0152% of elements' figure is **UNVERIFIED** (tensors not persisted) and is labelled so. TWO STUB PREDICTIONS FALSIFIED AND RECORDED: the 114688 VMEM-wall probe neither failed to compile nor SIGSEGV'd (88,989,696 B, 1.138x), and 'optima can sit below the default' found monotone degradation instead (-15%, -27%). sigma-gated ranking survived independently (65536 4.6580 +/- 0.0041 vs 114688 4.7151 +/- 0.0076, gap 1.226%, both orderings agree). Verifier ALSO CAUGHT AN ARITHMETIC ERROR the author had drafted — '851.7 TFLOP/s = 89.8%' mixes a device numerator with a wall percentage (it is 92.8%); corrected on-branch in `2d8b42a65` and now stated as two separate framings. **NOT AT CEILING: device time is still 1.074x the compute floor (~0.31 ms of the original 0.995 ms residual unclaimed) and BOTH enumerated `dense-near-roofline` levers are UNTRIED** — `custom-grid-block-matmul` has never been attempted, and `refute` is BLOCKED until a parity-PASSING hand kernel measurably loses => v002, which now must beat 4.6718 ms rather than 5.3437 ms. Disclosed gaps: author and verifier **serialized on the same chip 0** (chips 1-3 held by other families; the verifier's own `naive_default` leg carried mean 10.62 / std 51.07 against p50 5.344, implying one ~530 ms stall — p50 unaffected, gap real), **portability attestation NOT run**, and the 93.1% device figure rests on **n=2**. Plan coverage 9 declared / 9 executed / 0 abandoned + 6 recorded extensions. Receipts `raw/profiles/p6o5/gemm-v001/{flagfair_65536,leg_*,trial*,verify_full_65536,verifier}/` (supported).
- **ragged-paged-attention v020 opus5-process (2026-07-28)**: **REFUTED — page depth to the wall LOSES 18%, the stub's own registered COUNTER-PREDICTION fires, and the page's real product is a MODEL FAILURE the firing audit could not have caught: every predicted signal fired and the kernel is still slower.** Hypothesis: take page depth to its VMEM wall at `P=256` (`nblocks=1` ⇒ 64 programs, one per sequence, staging all 4096 keys), buying back MXU/DMA overlap (A18) while halving the online-softmax rescale's element-reads (A14); bar ≥1.03x vs the v011 frontier. **Verifier: 0.8198x** (`verifier/vs-frontier-receipt.json`, pooled n=146, orderings 0.8195/0.8201), a **21 pp miss**, reproduction 1.0129, **parity PASS both seeds** — so this is a bound-confirming refute from a PARITY-PASSING candidate, not an `inconclusive`. **THE FIRING AUDIT IS CONFIRMED ON EVERY REGISTERED SIGNAL AND THE MEASUREMENT CONTRADICTS IT**: grid `(64,2)→(64,1)`, `matmul_count` conserved grid-wide at 524,288, VMEM 22.1→38.1 MiB, and — the stub's PRIMARY signal, pre-registered as *'if overlap does not move the mechanism did not fire regardless of what p50 does'* — **`overlap_mxu_mem` ROSE 41.2→45.4% and `mxu_idle_stall_pct` FELL 55.6→52.4%, exactly as A18 predicted**. The candidate even issues **5.7% FEWER total static bundles**. Leg (b) confirmed the traffic IS spent: the tile is `f32[2048,512]` = 4 MiB with **no named allocation**, while the kernel carries **two untagged `u8[2097152]` scoped buffers = exactly 4 MiB of anonymous SPILL ARENA** (beside the named 16 MiB KV ring, 2 MiB `acc`, two 16 KiB `m`/`l`; 22.1 MiB total), against v013's 90.2% spill/fill share of vector-memory ops. **cand3's planted negative control was ALSO refuted**: NB=128 puts the PV contraction below the v6e MXU tile edge of 256 where the class page predicts a ~3.5x pipeline-fill collapse, and it measured 0.8307x — **beating NB=512's 0.8243x**. No collapse, no monotone edge penalty: at a VPU-serialized op-point the MXU-edge rule is not the binding term (the class rule isn't contradicted in general — it was earned where the contraction IS dominant). **TRANSFERABLE #2 — THE COST MODEL, from three manipulations of the SAME tile, and it supersedes v017's own "load traffic" framing**: v016 **skipped 87% of the ELEMENT-READS** of one pass => **+1.1620x**; v017 reshaped that pass's reduction tree while still reading every element => **-1.2%**; v018 shrank the buffer 8x so nothing materializes, still reading every element => **-8.0%**. **THE COST IS THE NUMBER OF ELEMENT-READS OF THE TILE SUMMED OVER PASSES, AND NOTHING ELSE** — not buffer size, not reduction shape, not register pressure (v017 measured spills RISING). Every future hypothesis about such a kernel must state WHICH ELEMENT-READS IT REMOVES; one that removes none will lose. That combination is the value: it isolates reduction-tree depth as a single variable, shows it buys nothing, and thereby RE-ATTRIBUTES the previous experiment's win.** Mechanism: keep the rowmax EXACT (`max` is exactly associative, so delta = 0 BY CONSTRUCTION, the output is bit-identical and the 2.0x `max_abs` gate that blocked v007/v015/v016 is **not in play at all**) and restructure only its SHAPE — 8 sequential 256-sublane sub-reductions folded into one `(M,)` running accumulator, which is `get_llo_fit_summary`'s rank-1 recommendation *verbatim* ("restructure the live-register set: less unroll / **split accumulator chain**"). **Result: c1024 0.9957x / c512 0.9890x / c256 0.9885x / c128 0.9858x / reshape-r256 0.9892x — every dose a REGRESSION, deepening MONOTONICALLY with chunk count**, which is the signature of a fixed per-chunk cost and the exact opposite of live-set relief. The LLO isolates it: only TWO mnemonics move, `vmax.f32` 8,264 -> 8,936 and `vrot.slane` 4,528 -> 5,200, **each by exactly +672 = 7 extra chunk-finalizations x 8 GQA groups x 12 ops** (a 3-step intra-vreg sublane rotate-and-max across 4 lane groups) — and half lands on the **2-slot XLU**. Spills 30,248 -> 31,075, fills 45,936 -> 46,751, bundles 39,267 -> 39,721, spills+fills/bundle 1.94 -> 1.96. **Splitting one reduction into N does not make the tree cheaper — it makes you pay the tree's TAIL N times. - **ragged-paged-attention v016 opus5-process (2026-07-27)**: **REFUTED — the mechanism fired more cleanly than anything this family has authored (29 of 30 LLO instruction mnemonics BIT-IDENTICAL, only `vmax.f32` moving 8,264 -> 1,096 = -86.7%), delivered MORE speed than its own pre-registered cap allowed, and failed the parity gate at EVERY dose that buys speed — which closes an entire CLASS rather than a route.** Mechanism: narrow the cross-sublane rowmax from the full `(nkey=2048, M=512)` tile to its first `SEEDN` sublanes — `jnp.max(scT, axis=0)` -> `jnp.max(scT[:SEEDN], axis=0)`, a ONE-LINE strict NARROWING of existing work (no norms, no extra matmul, no new tensor), A8-compliant by construction because `scT` is transposed at source so `axis=0` is the SUBLANE axis and the slice is free. **The safety argument was a THEOREM about the mask, not a numeric bound**: masked <=> `_j_idx > min(_q_span, kv_len-1)` with `_j_idx = base + iota(axis=0)` is MONOTONE in the row index, so each column's unmasked rows form a PREFIX `[0,R)`, and the sentinel (`-0.7*fp32max`) can NEVER become the shift while a real score exists in the same column because the seed is drawn from the LOWEST-indexed keys. cand0 (CPU-only fp64, zero chip time) confirmed the theorem to SEVEN significant figures — `max_exp_argument == delta_max` at all seed sizes, 32x overflow margin — and 12.7-25.2% of (column, block) pairs have delta EXACTLY zero. **THE DOSE-RESPONSE IS THE PAGE'S CONTRIBUTION** (5 doses x 4 seeds = 20 graded cells, plus a perfect adverse control): SEEDN=128 2.3902 ms **1.1796x** / 256 2.4309 ms 1.1620x / 512 2.4876 ms 1.1318x / 1024 2.6089 ms 1.0764x / 2048 (control) 2.8030 ms **1.0001x** with parity reproducing v011's banked ratios BYTE FOR BYTE — so the plumbing is free and no row is contaminated. Speed rises strictly as SEEDN falls, and **the FIRST dose off the control (SEEDN=1024, a mere 2x narrowing worth only 1.0764x) ALREADY BREACHES** at seed 44 (2.1164 > 2.0). Since there is no tighter approximate shift than an exact max over almost the whole tile, **A6 IS CLOSED AS A CLASS**: v015 killed Cauchy-Schwarz on a sqrt(D) geometric gap, v016 kills the sub-tile seed by dose-response, and nothing tighter exists. **TRANSFERABLE #1 — THE COST MODEL WAS WRONG ABOUT WHY, AND THIS CORRECTS v014**: the element-count model predicted 1.0952x and the measurement is 1.1620x; VALU absolute work fell only **7.7%** while bundles fell **12.5%**, because a shallower reduction tree also cuts **spill+fill traffic 26.7%** (76,184 -> 55,848), and the BUNDLE RATIO 1.1423 predicts the wall ratio 1.1620 to **1.7%** where the element model is off by 6.1%. **A reduction's cost is its element count PLUS the register pressure of its tree** — which promotes a new rank-1 lead that needs NO gate decision: compute the EXACT rowmax with a shallower two-level tree (delta = 0 by construction, so parity is bit-identical and the gate is not in play). **TRANSFERABLE #2 — BINDING INSTRUMENT RULE**: `occupancy_pct` is a per-bundle MEAN, so it must NEVER be compared pointwise across a mechanism that changes the bundle count. All three of this page's planted discriminators read as BREACHES on raw % (VALU 58.82->61.98, XLU 26.08->29.79, EUP 22.19->25.34) and ALL PASS on absolute issued slots (`% x capacity x bundles`): MXU 32,764->32,768, **XLU 20,482->20,482 an EXACT INTEGER MATCH**, EUP 8,713->8,711. Write discriminator thresholds in absolute slots, never %. **TRANSFERABLE #3 — BINDING CENSUS RULE**: a pre-authoring ULP census must replicate the kernel's OUTPUT dtype and its internal cast points or it prices a different kernel. cand0 predicted a max_abs ratio of 0.8276x and the chip delivered ~2.05x, because the census compared fp32 outputs while the kernel emits bf16 and casts `pT` to bf16 before the PV matmul — the entire gating error is a single 2^-9 bf16 quantum, invisible at fp32 resolution. (v015's census succeeded only because its perturbation was large enough to dominate quantization.) **THE INSTRUMENT CASE IS NOW THREE-DEEP**: v007, v015 and v016 have each been blocked by the same gate, and v016 measured it misbehaving directly — `max_abs` is **NON-MONOTONE in the perturbation size** (seed 45 reads 1.8989 at SEEDN=128 but 2.6427 at SEEDN=256, i.e. WORSE at the SMALLER delta) and swings **105%** across a dose range over which `mean_abs` moves **0.7%** (1.8612->1.8424, with the frontier itself at 1.8424). Between the frontier and ~24.4-24.8% of v6e bf16 peak stands one instrument decision, and a family may not make it. **The gate was NOT touched.** The kernel is structurally CORRECT (GQA group isolation 3/3 exact, causal 0 violations / 732 checks, ragged corners 1.0000x, all finite, interpret gate 44/44 with the bit-identity sub-prediction holding 2/2 on the `R <= SEEDN` cell) — it fails a tail statistic, not a semantics check. SEEDN=128 at 2.3902 ms and 256 at 2.4309 ms are the FASTEST kernels in this family's history (v007's blocked code was 2.480038), and none of them is bankable. - **mamba2-ssd v005 opus5-process (2026-07-27)**: **INCONCLUSIVE — THE MECHANISM FIRED HARDER THAN PREDICTED, BEAT ITS BAR BY 4x, AND CANNOT SHIP; and the page's real contribution is a METHODOLOGICAL CORRECTION TO HOW THIS WHOLE CAMPAIGN READS ITS BLOCKING CEILING DIAGNOSTICS: A CEILING CONTROL BOUNDS THE MECHANISM IT REMOVES, NOT EVERY MECHANISM THE CANDIDATE CONTAINS.** Took the family's LAST UNTRIED class lever (*bfloat16 MXU GEMM with fp32 accumulator tail*). K1 vs the NEW v004 frontier found a bigger target than the lead predicted: the intra-chunk score tile is `f32[4,64,16,256,256]` = **1.00 GiB = 62% of the frontier's 1.92 GiB** and 3.5x the next allocation — it GREW from 512 MiB when v004 moved the tile optimum 128->256, because the bank scales S*C. Its two readers want DIFFERENT WIDTHS: `o_intra` already rounds it to bf16 at the operand, so that consumer's fp32 half is written to HBM, read back and discarded; only `r_intra = sum(s,-1)` uses the width, for a REDUCTION the MXU accumulates in fp32 for free. MECHANISM: narrow the tile and recover the exact reduction as a COLUMN of the matmul that already exists (`sum(s,-1) == s@ones`), reusing the `vaug` ones-column v004 built for the inter-chunk term. **FIRING CONFIRMED AND EXCEEDED**: the 1.00 GiB tile has **NO ALLOCATION AT ALL** rather than the predicted halving to bf16 512 MiB, largest alloc drops to 288 MiB, temp 1,535,704,736 -> 914,915,008, **peak -736 MiB** (v004's peak had RISEN), convolutions 4->4. **1.4569x author / 1.4614x verifier co-measured, 10.8548x / 10.848x vs naive** — the family's largest number and past the 8.28x all-time reference — against a 1.12x bar. **AND IT FAILS PARITY.** The verifier ran 12 seeds on the candidate AND THE IDENTICAL SWEEP ON THE SHIPPED v004 FRONTIER AS A CONTROL: cand1 fails 7/12 with a **mean max_abs ratio of 2.213, above the 2.0 gate itself**, while the frontier fails 1/12 at mean 1.474. The family's documented seed-fragile-arm reading was TESTED AND REJECTED — that reading needs the candidate's own error flat while the floor moves, but cand1's own max_abs is 46% larger than the frontier's (0.0577 vs 0.0394) and its floor-error correlation is weak (r=-0.28). The control is not invalidated — its SCOPE is. ragged v014 and this family's own v002 and v004 all treated a cand0 as *the* cap; it is a cap ON ONE AXIS. **FINDING 2, PROVEN BY ACCIDENT: THE DELETION COMES FROM THE FOLD, NOT FROM bf16.** cand3b was written to keep the fold with an fp32 tile and returned temp AND peak AND parity ratios **BYTE-IDENTICAL** to cand1 — which is itself the diagnostic, since two candidates whose parity reproduces to the last digit compile to the same arithmetic: **on TPU `jnp.einsum`'s DEFAULT precision downcasts fp32 operands to bf16 on the MXU, and `preferred_element_type` sets only the ACCUMULATOR, never the operands.** So the page's own title, which credits bf16, is half wrong — recorded rather than quietly fixed. **FINDING 3: DELETION OR EXACTNESS, NOT BOTH, and it is structural rather than numerical** — single-consumer is what deletes the buffer, merging the two readers forces r_intra's 256-term reduction onto the MXU, and MXU operands are bf16 at any affordable precision: `precision=HIGH` and `HIGHEST` compile to the SAME decomposition here (byte-identical temp), both recover parity (cand3c max_abs 1.02-1.49, **mean-abs 1.4084-1.4097, the most accurate kernel in family history**) and both REGRESS to 0.8380x/0.8680x while RE-MATERIALIZING 160 MB above the frontier. No intermediate rung exists. Further: cand2 (narrow the scan's stacked output) is **STRUCTURALLY INERT**, temp byte-identical — the SECOND consecutive failure to make XLA drop the duplicated fp32 stacked state after v004 cand1b, so the `f32[16,4,64,64,65]` 128 MiB + `bf16` 64 MiB coexistence is **unreachable from the JAX level at this op-point**; cand4's tile re-derivation 1.1780/**1.4569**/1.2400 at C=128/256/512 shows the optimum **DID NOT MOVE**, refuting this page's OWN prediction and breaking a 3/3 streak (v002 unmoved, v003 inverted, v004 +1 step, v005 unmoved); and **the traffic->time conversion factor is NOT a constant** — v004 measured 57%, cand1 achieved **91.9%**, so the 1.12x bar priced off 57% was conservative by ~1.6x and future bars on this family should quote a RANGE. Two evidence-trail notes, both self-disclosed: the author attributed cand1's seeds 44/45 from cand3b's byte-identical `outputs_sha256` rather than running cand1 directly (sound inference, verifier closed it by direct run matching to 4 decimals — but **a number attributed by inference is not a measured number, even when the inference is correct**), and a first screen run was invoked from inside `.repo`, doubling every relative path, preserved as `receipt-INVALID-harness-path-error.json`. **MASTER DECISION REQUESTED, the 2nd family to reach this shape after ragged v007:** a 10.85x kernel that is MORE accurate than the shipped frontier on mean-abs (1.393 vs 1.680) is blocked by max-abs, the two arms disagree in opposite directions, the floor swings ~2x across seeds, and the verifier found a landmine seed (50) at which **the SHIPPED v004 frontier itself fails** (2.232) — reported upward, NOT acted on, since a family may not recalibrate its own gate. **CLASS-LEVER QUEUE NOW EXHAUSTED 8/8** => trigger for the family's FIRST retrospective, NOT a close (K9 needs two with candidate work between them); leads live: the **288 MiB f32 projection output, now the LARGEST allocation**, and the 128 MiB bf16 value-operand relayout (the 'fourth sink') => v006. - **mamba2-ssd v004 opus5-process (2026-07-27)**: **REFUTED — the mechanism fired completely and the page's real contribution is a NAMED, VERIFIER-CONFIRMED FAILURE MODE FOR STRUCTURAL REWRITES: A REWRITE CAN FORFEIT A COMPILER OPTIMIZATION THE BASELINE WAS SILENTLY RECEIVING, so the lever's PRICE and the rewrite's YIELD are different numbers and the gap is whatever the compiler was already doing for free.** K1 was run against the FRONTIER (`frontier_v002.py`), not the naive, and found the largest single avoidable item in a term nobody had looked at: the inter-chunk row-normalizer `r_inter` lowers at ENTRY level (not inside any fusion) as a 2 MiB VMEM state vector BROADCAST 128x into a 256 MiB HBM buffer, RELAYOUT-COPIED into a second 256 MiB HBM buffer, then reduced to 4 MiB — **~1.0 GiB of traffic to produce 4 MiB of output**, ~40% of the frontier's measured ~2.70 GB. Mechanism: carry an augmented `(D, D+1)` state `[Hstack | hstack]` through the existing `lax.scan` so the EXISTING projection contraction emits `o_inter` in `[...,:D]` and `r_inter` in `[...,D]` — free in tiled memory because `D=64` is ALREADY padded to the v6e 128-lane tile (measured: `bf16[32,4,64,64,64]` occupies exactly 2x its logical size). **FIRING CONFIRMED ON THE VERIFIER'S OWN FRESH DUMPS**, all four registered signals: frontier `%broadcast.4` `f32[4,64,32,128,64]` + ENTRY `%reshape.12` `f32[4,64,4096,64]` + `multiply_reduce_fusion` all present -> ALL ABSENT in the candidate; dots 4/4 (no MXU work added); projection minor dim 64->65. **AND IT MISSED ITS OWN >=1.20x BAR AT 1.0709x** (verifier, THREE fresh processes: 1.0697/1.0725/1.0705, agreeing to 0.3%). **THE DECISIVE INSTRUMENT IS cand0, A BLOCKING WRONG-MATH CEILING DIAGNOSTIC**: deleting the `r_inter` path outright (with `hstack` still consumed so the scan machinery is NOT DCE'd — verified dots 4->4, `while` 1->1, state mentions 23->23) is worth **1.2924x** and takes `temp_size_in_bytes` -49.6%, so ONE TERM IS HALF THE FRONTIER'S ENTIRE TEMP FOOTPRINT and **no rewrite of it can beat 1.2924x** — the fold therefore captured only **~23% of its own measured ceiling**, and the ceiling ALSO corrected the stub's bar model against itself (traffic->time converts at ~44%, so the pessimistic half-conversion model was right and the pre-registered 1.20x silently required capturing 69% of the deletable cost; **the bar was NOT moved after seeing this**). **CAUSE, CONFIRMED TO THE BYTE BY THE VERIFIER AND WORSE THAN THE AUTHOR DISCLOSED:** XLA self-demotes the frontier's stacked scan state to `bf16[32,4,64,64,64]` = 128 MiB (its only consumer is a default-precision einsum); the augmented form is `f32[32,4,64,64,65]` = 256 MiB and the demotion stops happening — and at the SHIPPED C=256 the `while` carry tuple holds **BOTH** an `f32[16,4,64,64,65]` (128 MiB) **AND** a `bf16[16,4,64,64,65]` (64 MiB) copy of the same logical state SIMULTANEOUSLY. ~1.0 GiB of broadcast traffic came out; a few hundred MiB of state/output traffic went back in. Two recovery attempts recorded rather than discarded: **cand1b** (bf16 stacked state from the scan body, fp32 carry) is **STRUCTURALLY INERT** — temp byte-identical, XLA did not sink the convert into the loop, abandoned pre-measurement with NO p50 claimed; **cand1c** (state-level fold point, which also restores `kvec`'s exact fp32 reduction) is **0.8506x, a 15% REGRESSION**, because the concat is a real copy materializing THREE 256 MiB state buffers, one of them an `f32[32,4,64,64,1]` holding **2 MiB of data in 256 MiB of tile**. **THE LANE-PADDING FACT POINTS BOTH WAYS AND THE FOLD POINT DECIDES WHICH YOU GET:** a `+1` column riding an existing 64-wide tile is free; a standalone 1-wide tensor costs 128x its content. **CLASS-PAGE CORRECTION, MEASURED NOT ARGUED (reported upward, not self-applied):** the lever *'Non-broadcast vector reshape `(...,1,C)`'* **DOES NOT TRANSFER FROM PALLAS TO XLA** — cand2 implemented it literally and is a **SILENT NO-OP called from the HLO BEFORE the clock**: `temp_size_in_bytes` = 1,082,326,816, the frontier's figure EXACTLY, every counter identical, 1.0001x. In Pallas the reshape is load-bearing because it changes what a `BlockSpec` fetches; in XLA a dot whose non-contracting output dim is 1 is canonicalized straight back into broadcast+multiply+reduce — the exact lowering the lever exists to avoid. **Expressing the intent is not enough; the op has to stop being a matrix-vector product.** **TILE OPTIMUM MOVED ONE LEGAL STEP, 128 -> 256** (C=64/128/256/512 = 0.4442/1.0538/**1.0687**/0.7523, both neighbours below it) — mechanistically consistent, since removing chunk-count-scaling inter-chunk traffic cheapens the axis that held the optimum down; **third structural change in this family, third different sweep shape** (v002 unmoved, v003 inverted, v004 one step up), so the class page's warning has fired 3/3 times here and never been wasted work. **FRONTIER ADVANCED ANYWAY to 7.5704x vs naive / p50 3.7591 ms**, `verify --mode full` all FOUR gates PASS with reproduction MATCHES (1.0016) — this family's SECOND `refuted`-mechanism / frontier-advanced pair, and it sits under the campaign's OPEN MASTER RULING on strict-own-bar accounting (3 instances). Parity is the page's other strong result: **mean_abs_ratio_vs_floor 1.6796-1.6816 across ELEVEN seeds vs the frontier's 1.9274-1.9279 — the shipped kernel is MORE ACCURATE than the one it replaces**, and its own error is seed-invariant to 4 significant figures while the max-abs RATIO swings 1.20-2.84x (adversarial seeds 777/2026 FAIL the max-abs arm at 2.3802/2.8382 — **floor variance, not candidate variance**, the v001 caveat reproduced on v004 by direct measurement). The verifier also KILLED a self-disclosed parity worry rather than inheriting it: the author flagged 2.6-6% divergence in a near-unit-decay regime; the verifier reproduced it (2.7-11.3%, so not overstated) and then showed it is **STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE** — `baseline.create_inputs` hardcodes `A_log = N(-4.0, 0.5)` as a literal, so reaching unit decay needs an 8-sigma excursion from a distribution whose mean is not seed-controllable, and at the REAL op-point 76.6% of output elements are bit-identical to the frontier with L2-rel 0.276%. TWO EVIDENCE-TRAIL DEFECTS CAUGHT BY THE VERIFIER AND CORRECTED IN PLACE: the author's peak-memory figure (1.29 GiB) does not reproduce — it is **1.42 GiB**, a ~130 MiB/10% understatement whose direction STRENGTHENS the finding; and the ledger's screen row cited `cand-screen/receipt.json`, **which does not exist** (the number reproduces twice independently, so an evidence-trail gap, not a fabrication) => earned process datum: **a ledger row's receipt path must be `ls`-checked at K7, not at close**. TOOL GAP REPORTED UPWARD (not self-fixed, `tools/kernelgate` is off-limits): `kgate verify-receipt`'s input-regen check is **unconditionally inapplicable to any `cmd=verify` receipt** because the verify schema never populates top-level `inputs_sha256` (`receipt.py:75-97`) — it advertises a guarantee it cannot deliver for the exact receipt class the Stop rule leans on hardest; recommended fix is to populate it from the nested measure/parity legs, which already carry it. Achieved HBM bandwidth **42.4% -> 60.4% of nominal**, MXU **1.46% -> 2.59%**. **NOT AT CEILING: exactly ONE of the routed class page's eight levers is untried — *bfloat16 MXU GEMM with fp32 accumulator tail* — and the forfeited demotion makes it BETTER-priced, not worse, since an explicit bf16-with-fp32-accumulator state is precisely what recovers it, on top of the 512 MiB score-tile bank it was already aimed at => v005.** Plan coverage 4 declared / 4 executed / 0 abandoned + 3 recorded extensions each with an inline reason. So **4.57 ms is a MEASURED program-count-free floor, not an extrapolation**: with per-program orchestration driven to ~0 the kernel is still 1.27x slower than the XLA it replaces. Two structurally distinct kernels (intra-only, fully-fused), four tile sizes and six blocking factors all land on the same wall. **TILE SENSITIVITY INVERTS UNDER THE KERNEL** (the class page's warning, observed live): C=128/256/512/1024 = 0.461/0.526/0.587/0.621x — monotone IMPROVING and asymptotic, exactly opposite to XLA's optimum at C=128; **C=1024 does EIGHT TIMES the intra-chunk arithmetic and is 35% FASTER**, which prices per-program cost as dominant over FLOPs by a wide margin. The fully-fused variant (intra tile + inter projection + normalizer + cast in ONE `pallas_call`, so the fusion barrier is crossed once) recovers ~11% — real but minor, and it **RULES THE BARRIER OUT as the explanation** for a 2.2x deficit. **FIRING AUDIT IS `PARTIAL`, NOT CONFIRMED, AND THE PAGE SAYS SO AGAINST ITSELF: 2 of 5 predicted signals FAIL.** (a) `temp_size_in_bytes` was predicted to FALL ~512 MiB and instead ROSE — a Pallas `custom_call` is a FUSION BARRIER, so `o_intra`/`r_intra` lost their downstream fusion and must be materialized; `get_device_wall_report` puts the kernel's OWN on-device time at just **1.80 ms of the 4.57 ms wall** (and disagrees with op_profile's custom-call bucket by ~5x — reported as an unresolved cross-tool discrepancy rather than reconciled by assumption). **THE KERNEL THAT LOSES IS THE MORE ACCURATE ONE:** mean-abs ratio vs the oracle floor **1.6802/1.6804** (kernel) vs **1.9274/1.9279** (frontier), verifier-confirmed to 4 significant figures, because the inter-chunk projection and row-normalizer stay fp32 in-kernel instead of round-tripping through bf16 — the route is capped on TIME, not numerics. **TWO PAGE-EVIDENCE ERRORS FOUND BY THE VERIFIER AND CORRECTED INLINE AS [!warning] BLOCKS, neither verdict-relevant, the second transferable:** (1) cand1's temp rise is **+2.8% (1.112 GB)**, not the **+27.6% (1.381 GB)** the page claimed — the verifier's instrument is calibrated, reproducing the frontier's `1,082,326,816` to the byte; NEW FAMILY (JAXBench 16p, Mamba-2-2.7B SSD, B4 H64 S4096 D64 bf16, `capability-eval`). K1 named ONE sink and the rewrite deleted it: the reference evaluates SSD in its **quadratic** form and round-trips a single `f32[4,64,4096,4096]` = 17.18 GB score buffer at 1.227 TB/s (75% of nominal) while using **4.2%** of the MXU. gqa v009 86.8% vs 86.5% baseline / attackable = 81% of bar / five authored arms best 1.0046x; here 98.08% vs 96.25% / attackable = 2% of bar) — strong enough to state as a rule: **on a fully-unrolled Pallas attention body whose score tile is many times the register file, register pressure is a COMPILER-VISIBLE SYMPTOM, NOT A TIME COST.** (Score tile here (1024,1024) fp32 = 4 MiB vs a ~256 KiB file; - **sparse-attention v010 opus5-process (2026-07-27)**: REFUTED — heads-per-program, the family's LAST program-count lever, CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT at every hp INCLUDING its maximal point, and the mechanism FIRED PERFECTLY while doing it. Bar was p50 <=0.7142 ms (>=5% over the 0.7518 ms verified frontier); best hp arm is 0.7632 ms (verifier), ABOVE the frontier. Frontier-tile row monotonically non-improving: **hp=1 1.000 | hp=2 1.000 | hp=4 0.981 | hp=8 0.881** — the LARGEST fold (a whole kv group in one program, the configuration the Mechanism paragraph claimed the most for) is the WORST. **THE FINDING, and it is the transferable one: `programs x emitted tpu.matmul` is INVARIANT at 1280 for every hp.** The grid collapses EXACTLY hp-fold (64/32/16/8, re-derived to the integer by a verifier-written census AND by hand from the kernel's control flow), but Pallas fully UNROLLS the folded heads into the body instead of sharing anything across them, so every program removed is repaid ONE-FOR-ONE in body size (601 -> 4313 lowered lines at hp=8 to delete 56 of 64 programs). That is the difference between 'the lever is small here' and '**the lever cannot exist in this form**' — the predicted saving requires SHARED work and the emitted program shares none. Generalizes past this op-point to any grid-collapse hypothesis on a fully-unrolled Pallas body. Both mechanism terms priced at ZERO independently: (b) K/V sharing died at the BLOCKING cand1 ablation BEFORE any hp machinery was written — scrambling the K/V block-index sequence to a stride-5 permutation costs 0.9987x (verifier 0.9972x), so Pallas's revisiting optimization already suppresses the re-fetch and there were never 64 loads to collapse to 8; (a) per-program overhead died on the verifier's device decomposition — device delta +18.0% tracks wall delta +14.0% at hp=8 and the off-device residue is FLAT across hp (0.151 vs 0.148 ms, matching v008's per-call-constant finding), so the whole hp effect is on-device compute, which does not shrink with hp. **PARITY-PASSING refute, so the bound is confirmed** (both seeds at 0.233x/0.261x of the naive's own fp32-oracle floor — candidate error BELOW the reference's — and every correct-math arm returns a BIT-IDENTICAL output sha256 to the frontier's). **FALSE-WALL DISCIPLINE PAID:** hp=4/hp=8 only reached the frontier tile because the 32 MB scoped-VMEM OOM (33.30M / 36.00M) was cracked with `--xla_tpu_scoped_vmem_limit_kib=65536` (both legs co-measured under the same flag); without it hp=8 — the maximal point — stays forever un-measured behind a false wall and the lever cannot be closed. THREE pre-registered predictions FALSIFIED and recorded as measurements: (i) the registered bq escape from the hp=8 OOM — the `kgate fit` map is NON-MONOTONIC in bq and hp=8/bq=2048 requests 53.47M, **17 MB WORSE** than the cell it was meant to rescue (reading, flagged as inference: second-axis double buffering exists only when bq<s, which is also why v009's argmin moved to bq=S and stayed); (ii) 'the tile argmin moves toward smaller bq as hp rises' — bq=4096 wins at every hp that fits; (iii) the predicted per-head scratch multiplicity — `scratch_shapes` is bit-identical at every hp (one acc/l pair reused SERIALLY), so the OOM was the q/out blocks, never the scratch. **NET GAIN BEYOND THE CLOSURE — lever-queue rank 7 (body size / Mosaic program-load) SIZED for the first time:** the hp arms are the controlled body-size experiment the family never had (constant total emitted arithmetic, constant op-point, bit-identical output, ONLY the programs-vs-body split moving), and at equal 2048 total emitted matmuls folding 4 heads into one body costs **1.87x**. Body size DOMINATES program count on this kernel — the OPPOSITE of the ordering v009's grid-collapse advance implied. Off the frontier tile it is a CLIFF not a slope (hp=4 vs hp=1 at the SAME tile: 0.535x at bq=2048, 0.511x at bq=1024; hp=2 at bq=2048 is 1.029x, the only positive reading anywhere, and it does not survive its tile). Verifier reproduced v009's L3 static digest BIT-FOR-BIT on the hp=1 arm (frontier kernel unchanged) and found hp folding WORSENS spill/fill (VSTORE:SPILL 30.2->32.8%, VLOAD:FILL 23.5->32.9%), foreclosing 'hp + spill-fix' as a compound lever. Frontier UNCHANGED at 0.7518 ms / 5.2807x. NOT at ceiling — v011 on the rank-3 scratch-layout/smaller-`bk` lead against the residual 30.2% spill (refuted).
- **ragged-paged-attention v001 opus5-process (2026-07-25)**: REFUTED with the MECHANISM FIRING COMPLETELY — a first experiment whose value is the wall decomposition, not the verdict. NEW FAMILY (JAXBench 7p, mixed prefill+decode: 64 q-tokens/seq + causal corner + GQA 64/8 + paged KV; distinct from the 6p `paged-attention` family, which is pure decode). Streaming online-softmax paged Pallas kernel verified at **3.5333x / 4.8672 ms** (verifier pooled n=158, both orderings 3.5301/3.5379; independent `kgate verify --mode full` 3.5215x; reproduction MATCHES 1.0026) against a PRE-REGISTERED >=4.0x bar => refuted, frontier ADVANCED (refuted-mechanism/frontier-advanced). **Every predicted structural signal hit EXACTLY on the verifier's own dump**: f32[64,64,4096] score tiles 144->0, bf16[4096,8,8,128] GQA repeat expansions 128->0, kCustom page-gather fusions 128->0, exactly 1 tpu_custom_call. **THE BAR CALIBRATION WAS THE ERROR AND THAT IS THE TRANSFERABLE LESSON**: 4.0x was derived from a SINGLE-TERM (bandwidth) envelope, but a 6-leg labeled wrong-math decomposition (verifier-reproduced within 0.5pp on all six) shows only 27.5% of the wall is DMA — and pure DMA already runs at ~84% of its own traffic floor. **A bar derived from a one-term envelope is unsafe on an op whose POST-transformation wall is multi-term**; the K1 envelope should have carried a VPU/layout term. Wall budget: 27.5% staging+transposes / **21.3% per-head sublane extraction** / 25.3% contractions+mask / 7.2% row sum / **3.9% exp** / **14.9% running-max machinery**. **THE TRANSCENDENTAL IS NOT THE WALL (3.9%)** — refutes the BRIEFS §2 'VPU issue slots are the second wall after streaming' prior at this op-point, and would have mis-aimed v002 toward const-shift-first. **HARD LAYOUT FINDING, transferable to every paged/combined-KV kernel**: `kv_pages` stores the 16 combined KV heads on the TILED SECOND-TO-LAST AXIS, so extracting one head is a sub-tile sublane slice — ILLEGAL on the DMA descriptor (Mosaic `Slice sizes along tiled dimensions must be aligned to tiles; size at dimension 2 not divisible by tile dimension 8`) and 21.3% of the wall when done in VMEM. The fix that DID work on the query side: pass q head-major so the GQA group slice is a free LEADING-axis slice (costs ~4.1% of device time in transposes; folding them in is a named lead). **FLAG-FAIRNESS GAP REPORTED (not edited)**: BRIEFS §1 says 'kernel-needs-flag => co-measure the naive WITH the flag', but here the scoped-VMEM flag that P=128 needs costs the REFERENCE 62% (17.22->27.91, verifier-confirmed), so the literal rule yields 6.055x — crediting the kernel with damage done to the baseline. Rejected it and banked the clean unflagged P=64 3.5333x; **the rule needs a naive-negative-flag clause**. Cheap ladder rungs were discharged BY MEASUREMENT before authoring (scoped-vmem regresses the naive monotonically 17.24->27.93; algebraic GQA repeat-fold only 1.2265x). Process: the VERIFIER's plan-coverage audit caught that declared cand5 was neither run nor abandoned-with-reason — closed with a measured envelope rule-out (group-in-grid multiplies whole-page staging 8x => >=5.32 ms staging alone). Second independent confirmation that **`kgate verify-receipt` cannot input-regen ANY verify-class receipt on this box** (schema gap: paths/hashes live in the nested child legs), matching the 6p family's finding — tool characteristic, NOT fabrication. NOT AT CEILING: 12.31% of peak vs a class yardstick calling streaming alone the STARTING point, with const-shift / split-loop / compute-pipelining all untried. Rank-1 v002 = kill the per-head sublane extraction via a VMEM->VMEM relayout AFTER landing (cand4 closed only the DMA-side route).

The blocking K1 (cand0) is the reusable half of the method: rather than inherit v003/v004's 18.0% mem-stall figure, it re-derived the bucket ON THE CURRENT FRONTIER (17.7%, MXU 75.76%, spills 1.60/bundle) and applied a decision rule the stub had PRE-REGISTERED (>=15% keeps the 1.05x bar; <10% would have made the honest hypothesis a refute filed without authoring anything) — so the bar could not be retrofitted to the result either way. THE MANDATORY CONTROL IS WHAT MAKES IT A PRICED REJECTION (v006's lesson applied): restructuring alone costs 1.0234x at identical arithmetic/depth/operands while the ENTIRE depth lever buys 1.0032x — a 7:1 penalty-to-gain ratio — and moving the buffering onto the stream that actually has depth (32-step q/out walk instead of a 1-4 block KV walk, at identical traffic) recovers only 0.9%, which answers the declared inherent-vs-KV-specific question: THE COST IS INHERENT TO `emit_pipeline`. Mechanism, flagged at cand0 BEFORE authoring: at 1.60 spills+fills per bundle much of the 'mem' in mem-stall is register<->VMEM spill traffic, which operand multi-buffering cannot touch by construction. TRANSFERABLE (campaign-level): `get_llo_fit_summary`'s ranked lever CEILINGS ARE HYPOTHESIS SOURCES, NOT HEADROOM ESTIMATES — its rank-2 [TUNE] entry advertised `<=1.22x` for this bucket and this family has now falsified that same estimate twice (v004 could not reach it, v007 reached it with the only capable API and got 0.3%). NOT at ceiling: 25.7% of the useful-FLOP envelope vs a ~45-50% class yardstick, verifier LLO verdict TUNE, and this experiment PROMOTES ITS OWN SUCCESSOR — the fit's rank-1 [STRUCTURAL] live-register-set/accumulator-chain split is now rank-1 for v008, both because v007 closed its rank-2 sibling and because the spill/fill evidence says the two are the same wall seen from two sides (refuted).
- **paged-attention v011 o5 (2026-07-25)**: the last coverage gap, and it closes. Tested by issuing N calls before one block_until_ready: the frontier per-call p50 goes 0.8816 / 0.8476 / 0.9477 / 0.9257 / 0.9642 / 0.9587 ms at N = 1/2/4/8/16/32 — it falls once then RISES, 0.92x at N=32 against a >=1.05x bar, the WRONG DIRECTION (pre-registered counter-signal (ii)). With this, every term of the wall is measured against a floor under every protocol the family can construct (refuted).
- **paged-attention v010 o5 (2026-07-25)**: mask-as-MXU-bias REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT, 0.7401x/0.7357x — a 26% regression on two structurally distinct, parity-clean (bit-identical in interpret at uniform AND ragged kv_lens), firing realizations. NOT refuted by VMEM: the 65536 KiB RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED (79.97 M requested vs 64.00 M limit) was read per BRIEFS STEP-0 and cracked at 98304 KiB, and the 0.7401x is flag-fair with both legs there. The generalizable lesson: v008 cand0's 'the MXU is 97.3% idle' is true but the slack is UNSPENDABLE here, because the bias contraction emits a full-size (64,32768) fp32 tile that must then be ADDED to the scores — one select over 2.1 M elements becomes a padded matmul PLUS an add over the same 2.1 M elements. RELOCATING ELEMENTWISE WORK TO THE MXU DOES NOT PAY WHEN THE RESULT IS A FULL-SIZE TILE THAT MUST STILL BE COMBINED ON THE VPU. cand1-vs-cand2 differing by 0.6% puts the whole loss in the contraction, none in operand construction. This CLOSES the third and last named direction on the mask (build cheaper = free-and-worthless v009; apply cheaper = unsafe v009; relocate = 26% loss v010). Family NOT at ceiling by the K9 chain — it has no retrospective at all; next step is /create-retrospective with a lever-coverage table, not another candidate. Newly named untried axis: every experiment has measured a SINGLE ISOLATED CALL, so the 0.1336 ms fixed dispatch term (15% of wall) is 'untouchable' only under that protocol — a pipelined deployment could overlap dispatch N+1 with compute N (refuted).
- **paged-attention v009 o5 (2026-07-25)**: the registered mechanism refuted and the interesting finding is a CORRECTNESS one. cand0 priced the validity mask at 0.0394 ms (ceiling 1.0465x, gate did not fire). cand3 — the page's hypothesis, rebuilding the grid-invariant mask term from small broadcast operands instead of two full-tile int32 iotas — measured 1.0006x with orderings straddling unity: Mosaic was ALREADY hoisting the invariant, the stub's pre-registered counter-signal (i), so the mask's cost is not in its construction. cand2 found it in the consumption (select -> multiply, 1.0228x co-measured, three bootstrap CIs all excluding 1.0) but FAILS the coverage battery with NaN: corrupted out-of-range tokens overflow exp() to +inf and inf*0.0 = NaN leaks into valid outputs, where the frontier's where(valid, exp, 0.0) discards the inf and passes. THE SELECT IS LOAD-BEARING — it buys overflow tolerance on out-of-contract data for ~2.5% of the wall, an exchange rate nobody had priced. Filed inconclusive (coverage FAIL caps it there; and calling the term closed would invite a premature at-ceiling claim when 0.0394 ms is demonstrably reachable). Frontier unchanged at v008. Rank-1 v010: fold the mask into the QK contraction as a rank-n_kv additive bias — rides the MXU slack v008 measured AND gives exp(score-BIG)=0 with no inf*0 hazard (inconclusive).
