Cauchy-Schwarz spends 20.17% of a 13.75% budget, and **cannot be tightened into it**, because its slack is a **sqrt(D) geometric gap**, not a resolution artifact (which is also why cand2, per-sub-tile `max||k||`, was abandoned with reason: it attacks the wrong term). **METHOD DATUM, second consecutive page:** v014 aborted on price, v015 predicted its own failure value — **pricing the DECLARED RISK before authoring produced the decisive number both times.** Proposed class-page wording: **when a mechanism's open question is accuracy rather than speed, the blocking diagnostic is an fp64 census of the proposed shift against the exact one, and its output is a COMPOSED ratio against the frontier's existing gate headroom, not against the gate.** **NOT at ceiling.**

- **ragged-paged-attention v014 opus5-process (2026-07-27)**: **refuted — the abort rule fired on the FIRST diagnostic (the whole online-softmax rescale chain is worth 0.9%), and the consequential half is that it RE-TARGETS v007, this family's largest blocked lever, by measuring a component v007's own page credited for its win.** Hypothesis: block-local softmax (own block max, no running-max carry, no accumulator rescale) with ONE deferred cross-block combine, framed as **the LEGAL route to v007's prize** — v007 deleted the same term via a constant shift and is blocked on a master parity decision because that changes the arithmetic, whereas exact block maxima plus an exact combine perform FEWER rescalings and should be equal-or-better in error. **cand0 BLOCKING DIAGNOSTIC** drops `corr` from BOTH accumulators (the corr exponential, the `(M,)` `l` multiply, and the **(M=512,D=128) fp32 `acc` READ-MODIFY-WRITE, 8x per program**) while leaving the block max, the exp, the mask bias and BOTH matmuls as shipped: **1.0087930x** pooled vs the v011 frontier (n=151, orderings 1.0088155/1.0087644, rel_diff 0.005%, PASS, anomalies []). Against a 3% abort threshold and a 1.05x bar the chain **cannot fund the bar under perfect execution** — NO KERNEL AUTHORED, cand1-cand5 abandoned by the pre-registered gate. **THE BOUND IS VALID DESPITE THE CONTROL BEING PARITY-INVALID, AND THE DIRECTION IS WHY**: the leg removes **strictly more** than any legal candidate could, so it bounds the mechanism **from above** — a correct block-local formulation must do at least this much work and can buy at most 0.9%. **Parity-invalidity weakens a WIN claim; it STRENGTHENS a CAP.** Confirmed invalid deliberately (not discovered, as in v013 cand0): the family battery FAILS 18 cells and `outputs_sha256` differs. **THE RE-TARGETING:** v007 credited its 1.327372x / 6.861084x-vs-naive / 23.89%-of-peak win to the acc rescale PLUS a cross-sublane rowmax PLUS a delta-exp. The rescale is now measured at **0.9%**, so **v007's win is predominantly the ROWMAX** — a **~1.05M-element cross-sublane reduction per group** (8.4M per program) against the rescale's 65,536, a **16x** ratio. Independent arithmetic agrees: the **per-element score-tile work** (bias + max + exp + subtract + sum, ~5 passes over 1.05M elements x 8 groups x 128 programs ~= **5.4 G element-ops** at ~3.85 T/s) is **~1.4 ms of a 2.817 ms kernel**, exactly what **VALU=58.82%** measures. **The VALU bound is the per-element score-tile work, not the accumulator bookkeeping.** Verdict is the **v009 shape** — one diagnostic, zero authored candidates, lever closed before a line of kernel code — and unlike v012/v013 (both `inconclusive` because their mechanisms never fired) **no firing question arises**: the chain WAS deleted, more completely than legally possible, and the clock moved 0.9%. **CLOSES** the rescale chain and every 'reorganize the accumulator bookkeeping' variant at a 1.009x cap. **OPENS** a NEW FAMILY-AUTHORITY route needing no gate change: delete the full-tile cross-sublane max while keeping a shift tight enough for the existing 2.0x gate — a **coarse blockwise max** (one sub-tile or a strided subsample), vastly tighter than a global constant yet still removing the 1.05M-element reduction => v015. **METHOD DATUM — the pre-authoring gate paid for itself on its FIRST application.** v013 earned *before authoring a mechanism that removes work, confirm from the emitted program that the work is there*; v014 generalized it to *...and price it*, and the price killed a page that would otherwise have cost five candidates. Proposed class-page wording: **a blocking OVER-REMOVAL control is the cheapest possible refutation — it bounds from above, so its parity-invalidity is a feature, not a defect.** NOT at ceiling.

- **ragged-paged-attention v013 opus5-process (2026-07-27)**: **inconclusive — THE REGISTERED MECHANISM WAS ALREADY PERFORMED BY THE COMPILER, so the hypothesis was never tested; and the blocking diagnostic ACCIDENTALLY EXPOSED A BENCHMARK-OVERFITTING TRAP THAT EVERY AUTOMATED GATE IN THIS CAMPAIGN WOULD HAVE CERTIFIED.** Hypothesis: the staging ring calls `_copies_g()` TWICE per program at entry — 256 `make_async_copy` builds each with an SMEM page-table read, half of them PURELY to derive wait handles — with every compute instruction downstream of the `wait()`, so nothing overlaps them; v012's LLO dump shows the consequence as one unbroken **6113-bundle `salu_bubble` run at bundle 0x0** (15.6% of the program) with SALU occupancy only **8.11%**, i.e. dependency serialization rather than scalar saturation. Removing the wait half should take the run to <=4200 and beat the v011 frontier by >=1.04x. **cand0 BLOCKING DIAGNOSTIC** (1 contiguous descriptor per program instead of 128): **1.0734x** pooled (n=204, orderings 1.0722/1.0739) => the pool is **7.34%**, abort rule does not fire. **Calibration datum: the LLO static estimate OVER-PRICES it** — `salu_bubble` 10.8% implies a 1.121x cap and the digest's rank-3 line says <=1.12x, against a measured 1.073x (4.5 pp / ~1.5x relative). Same direction as v006's 35x multi-buffering miss, two orders milder: **read static bundle-class shares as upper bounds, not prices.** **THE INTEGRITY FINDING, and it is the page's largest product: cand0 was pre-registered PARITY-INVALID BY CONSTRUCTION AND IT IS NOT.** `outputs_sha256` came back IDENTICAL to the frontier, because `baseline.py:49` builds `page_indices = jnp.arange(total_num_pages).reshape(max_seqs, pages_per_seq)` — **the JAXBench page table is a perfectly contiguous identity mapping**, so 'copy the P pages starting at this block's first index' IS the gather the page table asks for. Consequence: **a kernel that collapsed the 128 per-page descriptors into one contiguous copy would measure 1.073x, pass kgate parity BIT-IDENTICALLY, pass the firing audit, pass forbid-shape and every `matmul_count`/grid adverse control this family runs — and be catastrophically wrong for every real paged-attention workload, which exists precisely because the KV cache is NOT contiguous.** PROVEN not asserted: the same leg through the family's own non-contiguous battery (`tiny_gate.py`, page table = `rng.permutation(NP)`) **FAILS at 287-312x the oracle floor** on uniform-full AND ragged inputs at every P and NS. **The safeguard that catches it is the family's OWN non-degenerate probe, NOT `kgate parity` vs `baseline.py`** — BRIEFS's non-degenerate-probe rule, earned again the expensive way. DO NOT AUTHOR; recorded as a permanent red flag. **cand1 THE REMOVAL** (one aggregate wait descriptor replaces P reconstructed ones; `wait()` binds `dma_wait_p` on the descriptor's `dst_ref` so one over the whole slot waits for the same bytes): **1.0027x**, bit-identical, independent fresh-process re-measure **1.003x** — **and the two-leg LLO firing audit says it removed NOTHING**: `dma.done.wait` **4 in BOTH legs**, `dma.` **777 in both**, `salu_bubble` **10.8% -> 10.8%**, entry run **6113 -> 6115 bundles (it GREW by 2)**, SALU **8.11 -> 8.12%**, total bundles **-0.06%**. **The shipped kernel never emitted 128 waits — Mosaic had already coalesced them to 4, and the descriptor objects around them are dead code it already eliminates. The stub's 256-descriptor premise is FALSE AT THE INSTRUCTION LEVEL.** cand1a shape-only control 0.99988x confirms the plumbing reshape is free. **cand2 THE RELOCATION** (defer the lookahead issue past the group loop): **0.821x, a 22% REGRESSION**, bit-identical, +0.619 ms. The kernel reads **exactly 1 GiB** of KV once; 1 GiB at v6e's 1.6 TB/s nominal is 0.64 ms, so **the deferral exposes essentially the ENTIRE KV read (within ~3%)**. **cand3** NS sweep rescues it exactly — **0.821 / 1.002 / 1.002x at NS=2/3/4**, saturating one slot later than the shipped kernel, the signature of a pure lead-time deficit — but the rescue is a **WASH** costing +8 MiB. **TWO ENVELOPE FACTS THE FAMILY DID NOT HAVE, both from cand2: the staging ring is worth ~0.62 ms / ~18% of wall** (measured by REMOVING its overlap; v006 only ever priced ONE exposed block at 0.198397 ms), and **THE KERNEL IS NOT BANDWIDTH-BOUND, BY MEASUREMENT** — the engine moves 1 GiB in ~0.62 ms so it is busy only **~22% of wall**, average demanded bandwidth **381 GB/s ~= 24% of nominal**. That **retires the digest's rank-2 'deepen multi-buffering <=1.64x' as a PHANTOM** at this op-point from the opposite direction to v006, consistent with the K1 finding that **90% of the vector memory ops the tool counts as 'mem' are register spill/fill** (`VSTORE:SPILL` 38.52 of 40.5; `VLOAD:FILL` 38.99 of 44.68). **COMPILER FACT EARNED: the scheduler does NOT freely hoist compute above a side-effecting DMA start** — textual position of a DMA ISSUE is semantically load-bearing where pure VALUE reordering (v012 route B) is not. The stub's LOW prior for cand2 was right for the wrong reason and both halves are recorded. **VERDICT `inconclusive` by the stub's own pre-registered clause** (a parity-passing miss is `refuted` ONLY IF the firing audit shows the scalar work left the prologue; it did not). **CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT: v009's lead 3 SPLITS** — the 'hoist scalar address math' half is **~0.27 pp of a 7.34 pp pool** and largely compiler-eliminated already, while the 'batch DMA descriptors' half is **~7.07 pp** and is gated on the contiguity trap rather than on effort; plus relocation (a wash) and multi-buffering depth (second independent direction). **TRANSFERABLE RULE, THIRD CONFIRMATION IN ONE FAMILY** (v012 route A store-to-load forwarding, v012 route B trace-unrolled reorder, v013 cand1 dead-code-eliminated wait descriptors): **BEFORE AUTHORING A MECHANISM THAT REMOVES WORK, CHECK THE EMITTED PROGRAM TO CONFIRM THE WORK IS THERE** — one LLO dump plus one op-census grep, cheaper than a candidate, and it belongs in the K3 stub as a pre-authoring gate rather than in the K6 audit as a post-mortem. Plan coverage 6/6 executed (cand4 folded into cand2/cand3 with its reason), one extension (the firing audit), 5 candidate commits. 4th reproduction of the campaign-wide `xprof-cli get_llo_fit_summary` TypeError (`cli/main.py:72-73` str-coerces every numeric arg). **NOT at ceiling.** Rank-1 = nkey-slice the softmax+PV chain (v012's un-pulled rank 1, now carrying v013's pre-authoring gate); **rank-2 NEW = per-item software pipelining of the QK^T->exp->PV chain** — the class page's own prescription for a VPU-serialized wall (VALU 58.82%, MXU 41.72%, MXU-idle 55.6%) and never authored here, now that v013 has shown the DMA side is a solved and idle resource.

- **ragged-paged-attention v011 opus5-process (2026-07-27)**: **SUPPORTED — NEW FRONTIER and the family's all-time best: 2.990613 -> 2.817589 ms, 1.061449x over v008, 6.047187x vs naive, 21.02% of v6e bf16 peak — bought with a TWO-LINE DIFF on the CHEAPEST rung of the escalation ladder, after seven experiments spent on the most expensive one.** The family had read `37.09M requested vs 32.00M limit` as a hardware wall since v001 and engineered around it for v004-v010. - **ragged-paged-attention v009 opus5-process (2026-07-27)**: **REFUTED — the lever CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT before a line of kernel code, for FOUR `kgate measure` calls and ZERO authored candidates (the v004 shape), and the page's most valuable output is a correction to its OWN pre-registered check that the VERIFIER, not the author, reported.** Hypothesis: fold `k` KV sub-blocks into one Pallas program (grid `(S, nblocks/k)`, staging granularity and score-tile width held FIXED) so the 2 MiB fp32 `(m,l,acc)` state stops round-tripping VMEM scratch 256 times — the way to reach the P=128 operating point WITHOUT the tile widening that put it 5.09M over the 32 MiB budget. **THE PREMISE WAS CONFIRMED FROM SOURCE AND THE MAGNITUDE FAILED**: the verifier read the kernel and confirmed `m`/`l`/`acc` are `(Hkv, G*tps)` / `(Hkv, G*tps, D)` — depending only on `tps`/`G`/`Hkv`/`D`, **never** on `P`/`nkey` — and are read+written every grid step, so the fold really would remove `(256 - 256/k)` state round-trips; but per-program cost is bounded at **<=0.1451 us**, capping the entire fold at **1.006-1.019x (k=2) / 1.009-1.029x (k=4)** against a pre-registered **1.04x** abort threshold and a **1.03x** bar. **Authoring could not have produced a `supported` verdict under ANY attribution** — which is exactly what a blocking diagnostic exists to discover. **METHOD, and it is reusable**: the shipped kernel already exposes staging `P` and programs = `64*256/P`, so the EXISTING code sweeps program count with no new kernel (P=64/32/16/8 => 256/512/1024/2048 programs), each co-measured in-process against the frozen frontier. **THE MARGINAL COST IS NON-MONOTONE — 2.14 -> 0.145 -> 0.341 us/program** (13-20 sigma and ~81 sigma on the two deltas, independently reproduced) — which **alone** refutes the fixed-per-program-cost model the hypothesis rests on, since a fixed cost gives a CONSTANT marginal. **TOTAL DMA DESCRIPTOR COUNT IS INVARIANT BY ALGEBRAIC IDENTITY**: `P` copies/program x `S*(PPS/P)` programs = `S*PPS` = **16,384 for every P**, so none of the 0.96 ms spread from P=64 to P=8 is descriptor count. **THE SELF-AUDIT MISS, RECORDED BECAUSE THE PROCESS DEPENDS ON IT**: the diagnostic's own `workload_p8` docstring pre-declared 'a much larger rise would mean the bound logic is wrong' with a <=0.137 ms ceiling; observed **+0.355 ms, a ~2.5x overshoot at ~81 sigma**. The author's commit tabled the number and called the pattern 'non-monotone' but **never said its own falsification criterion had tripped** — the verifier caught it. The bound survives (the P=32->P=16 decomposition is logically separable and every attribution still clears 1.04x), but the honest headline is a **RANGE, 1.01-1.03x**, not the crisp sub-1.01x the flattest segment alone suggests. **A STANDING INFERENCE IN THIS FAMILY'S RECORD IS CORRECTED**: v006's verifier attributed the P=16 dose-response regression to a per-program scalar descriptor chain 'paid 4x more often' (self-labeled INFERENCE, since v6e exposes no runtime `sync_wait` counter). The descriptor count never changed, and the per-program term explains **at most 25%** of that cell's +0.605 ms even at the bound's ceiling — so the regression is dominated by the **exposure/pipelining** term, where v006's own dose-response pointed before the attribution was layered on top. Corroborated by LLO: the scalar-hoist ceiling is **flat at ~1.10-1.11x across P=64/32/16**, i.e. P-invariant, which also **de-prioritizes family lever 2 without spending a candidate on it**. **NEW INSTRUMENT GOTCHA reported upward**: `xprof-cli get_llo_fit_summary`/`get_llo_static_utilization` throw `TypeError: slice indices must be integers` EVEN WHEN `--hot_ranges`/`--top_stalls` are passed explicitly (Fire leaves them as strings); calling `llo_dump_tools.get_llo_fit_summary(...)` directly in Python bypasses it. **NOT at ceiling** — this page removes a lever from the queue rather than approaching a floor. With the fold closed and lever 2 de-prioritized, the **rank-0 blocker** (v007's unbanked **1.327372x / 23.89%-of-peak** kernel, a master decision about a shared instrument) is now larger than every surviving kernel-internal lever **COMBINED**, by roughly an order of magnitude.

- **ragged-paged-attention v008 opus5-process (2026-07-27)**: **SUPPORTED — the family's first `supported` since v003, the first frontier here bought from OUTSIDE the kernel body, and the cleanest cap-attainment it has recorded (99.5% of a cap that was MEASURED before authoring).** Fold the qT/oT transposes out of the host wrapper: the two whole-tensor relayouts around the `pallas_call` (`data formatting` 0.2013 ms / 64 MiB + `loop fusion` 0.0918 ms / 128 MiB = **0.2931 ms fixed per invocation = 8.9% of the v006 wall**, identical on every leg ever measured) are DELETED. Verifier full-mode receipt `verdict: PASS`: **2.990613 ms / 1.097291x vs the v006 frontier / 5.689715x vs naive / 183.83 TFLOP/s / 19.81% of v6e bf16 peak** (+1.73 pp), against a pre-registered **>=1.04x** bar and a pre-measured **1.0978x** perfect-elimination cap; author reproduced to **1.0000187**, the tightest in this family. **THE WINNING ROUTE WAS ELIMINATION, NOT TRANSFER, AND THAT IS THE TRANSFERABLE LESSON**: cand1/cand2 built the page's registered *in-VMEM transfer* mechanism (bulk `jnp.transpose` into a scratch under `pl.when(t==0)`) and reached only 1.019140x / 1.080117x, while **cand3 — pre-registered in the same plan as 'the structural alternative with NO in-kernel relayout at all' and predicted LIKELY TO FAIL** against the tiled-second-to-last-axis constraint that killed v002 cand2/cand2b — simply declares BOTH query and output BlockSpecs in the **native (T,Hq,D) layout**, deletes both VMEM scratches, and **lowers**. A layout tax can be removed by *not paying it* rather than by *moving it*, and the cheap route was the one the stub thought would not compile. **FIRING CONFIRMED TWO INDEPENDENT WAYS**: both ops absent from the candidate's HLO module (a 44-line ENTRY: 6 params -> one scalar copy -> ROOT custom-call reading native `bf16[4096,64,128]` and producing it as ROOT) AND absent from *measured device time* (`get_top_hlo_ops` 90.6% -> 99.9% custom-call); non-kernel gap **0.461002 -> 0.157522 ms**, landing on the predicted ~0.16 ms residual host dispatch. **THE COST-SIDE SUB-SIGNALS DID NOT MATERIALIZE, WHICH IS THE POINT**: device-busy +0.076%, XLU raw +0.00%, VMEM **byte-for-byte identical** at 14,786,560 B / 44.1%, `matmul_count` +0.00%, spills+fills 1.71/bundle unchanged — the cost model was written for the more expensive of two registered routes. Output **BIT-IDENTICAL** (sha256 equal both seeds, max_abs exactly 0.0) — a REQUIRED outcome for a permutation, backed by a **new GQA group-isolation probe** (perturbing kv-group g must move exactly q-heads [8g,8g+8) and nothing else: 3/3 with live positive controls), the instrument a max-abs gate is worst at. **cand4 INVERTS THE STUB'S OWN NOTE**: the shipped cand3 carries neither scratch, so the P=128/NS=2 deficit is 5.09M — **byte-identical to what v003 cand5 measured before any of v008's work** — this page costs the P=128 lever NOTHING; and NEW, **P=128 DOES fit at NS=1** (page depth and ring depth trade inside the same 32 MiB budget), the rank-1 v009 lead and a different mechanism, so not claimed here. **THREE FINDINGS REPORTED UPWARD**: (a) the frozen bar file `frontier_v008.py` **cannot serve as kgate's parity ORACLE source** — `--oracle auto` fp32-recasts the BASELINE entry and the native-layout kernel then needs 33.61M > 32.00M scoped VMEM (`frontier_v006.py` did not; NEW at v008; workaround pinned in the bar file's docstring: run parity against the naive `baseline.py`, which is what every gate v001-v008 actually rested on); (b) **`kgate verify-receipt` reports STATUS MISMATCH on EVERY `cmd=verify` receipt in this campaign** ('receipt lacks baseline path or input hashes'), including v006's banked one — a tool gap, not fabrication, since the hashes DO exist in `report.json`'s parity leg, but it makes every verify receipt look like a validation failure to LINT's fabricated-receipt check; (c) **the mandated `flock` + `TPU_VISIBLE_DEVICES` wrap is INSUFFICIENT under concurrent multi-family campaigns** — a sibling family's process on chip 0 blocked chip 1 until `TPU_PROCESS_BOUNDS=1,1,1 TPU_CHIPS_PER_PROCESS_BOUNDS=1,1,1` were added (per-chip flock cannot help: the collision is libtpu's PJRT client taking the node's whole chip set), which is also the confirmed cause of the predecessor session's all-gates-FAIL receipt. **NOT at ceiling**: the STRUCTURAL/register-pressure bound is untouched (this page attacked a host-side tax, not the kernel body), rank-1 kernel lever = **P=128 at NS=1 (v009)**, and the **rank-0 blocker remains v007's unbanked 1.327372x / 23.89%-of-peak kernel**, worth more than every remaining kernel-internal lever combined and a master decision about a shared instrument.

- **ragged-paged-attention v007 opus5-process (2026-07-27)**: **inconclusive — and the verdict is a statement about the INSTRUMENT, not the kernel. A fully-confirmed 1.327372x wall / 1.40116x DEVICE / 6.861084x vs naive / 23.89% of v6e bf16 peak candidate is blocked by a parity gate whose pass/fail on it is a seed lottery.** Const-shift softmax: the running max is replaced by a compile-time constant folded into the existing additive bias tile (so the shift itself is FREE), which deletes per group per program a cross-sublane rowmax, one delta-exp, `l*alpha` (512 fp32) and `acc*alpha` (65,536 fp32) — 524,288 fp32 multiplies per program x 256 programs. **Firing CONFIRMED on every registered signal** (bundles −24.0% vs a predicted ≥8%, VALU raw −9.14%, MXU raw and matmul_count +0.00%, `mem_stall_mxu_idle` 42.8→18.3%, `overlap_mxu_mem` 32.9→53.0%, MXU occupancy +11.9 pp) **including the discriminator the stub planted to catch a wrong deletion** (EUP raw −0.17% while bundles fell 24% ⇒ the main exp survived and only the delta-exp went). **Unpredicted bonus: spills −36.9%, fills −40.3%, spills+fills/bundle 1.71→1.37** — the first improvement to this family's register-pressure signature in four experiments, because `acc*alpha` was what kept the accumulator live. **THE TRANSFERABLE FINDING is a parity-instrument failure mode, established four ways:** (1) the kgate `max_abs` ratio is a SEED LOTTERY — 2.0625 / 1.2293 / 1.3262 at seeds 42/43/44, straddling the 2.0 gate — while `mean_abs` is stable to three decimals (1.0147/1.0149/1.0146); The shipped configuration is a local optimum in that dimension, and the pair is worth more than either page alone. **BAR-SETTING PRECEDENT WORTH REUSING:** the verifier ranked this lever #1 in both v002 and v003 citing `get_llo_fit_summary`'s **<=1.68x** TUNE estimate; the page REFUSED that as its bar on **ragged-paged-attention v006**'s measured precedent (same tool, same deepen-multi-buffering recommendation, same `mem_stall` metric: predicted <=1.75x, DELIVERED 1.055x, metric returned byte-for-byte unchanged after the real win) and calibrated instead from the two MEASURED neighbours (1.055x DMA-side, 1.316x class-page compute-side). **A verifier cannot apply that prior -- the Load mandate keeps the retrospectives digest master-side -- so re-ranking a verifier lead on cross-family measured evidence is the master's job, not an override to avoid.** **TWO VERIFIER CORRECTIONS ADOPTED, NOT ARGUED AWAY:** (1) cand6's "no effect" is re-stated -- the kv-axis interleave is a wall-clock non-effect (+-0.2 pp, sign-unstable between runs) but NOT an instruction-level no-op; at kv_split=2 it produces a real, FAVOURABLY-directed static restructuring (bundles -324, MXU occ +0.53 pp, mem-stall 37.2->35.9%) whose benefit never clears the noise floor, so the honest phrasing is "fires structurally, sub-noise realized benefit"; (2) a documentation bug of this page's own -- the docstring's "bk=2048 costs 0.4%" used the **V001 FRONTIER** as denominator, where against the current base it costs **~8%** (0.9209x, which the verifier reproduced directly on the PRE-refactor entry) -- which settles a discrepancy the page had flagged as a possible kv_split-refactor perturbation: it is a **STALE DENOMINATOR**, so v003's tile-axis closure is unaffected. Corrected in the CODE as well as the page (the prior K7's auditor DRIFT finding was exactly this code/page divergence class). **PROCESS NOTE worth transferring:** the first cand2/3/4 sweep produced NO receipt because the artifact dir did not exist AND the invocation piped kgate through a grep narrow enough to hide the error -- 12 numbers on screen, all uncitable. Re-run after mkdir. Two cheap rules: create the artifact dir before the run, and never filter a kgate invocation's stderr down to the rows you expect. Frontier unchanged at v001 (2.3316x); nothing banked; parity PASS both seeds, firing CONFIRMED, reproduction MATCHES (2.5394x author / 2.5398x verifier on the unchanged base). **NOT at ceiling:** 34.6% of the bf16 MXU envelope. Rank-1 lead => v005: **compact causal live-pair grid** -- after three experiments closing placement (v002), sizing (v003) and scheduling (v004), it is the only untried lever that removes WORK rather than rescheduling it, and 25-37.5% of programs are still dead-but-launched.
- **flex-attention v005 opus5-process (2026-07-27)**: **refuted ON A PRICED CEILING WITHOUT BUILDING THE MECHANISM — and the methodology is the transferable result, not the number.** Hypothesis: replace the rectangular `(H,n_q,n_kv)` grid with a compact live-pair grid so the **384 of 1024 (37.5%) causally dead-but-launched** programs are never launched; bar >=1.06x over the base. **The plan deliberately INVERTED the usual order**: cand1 priced the lever's entire ceiling BEFORE any compact grid was authored, with a pre-registered abort rule ("ceiling < 1.03x => refute here, on a measured bound"). **Probe design**: extend the grid's kv extent by `pad_kv` blocks while touching **NO TENSOR** — every BlockSpec index map already clamps through `live(i,j)=min(j,_last_live_kv(i,bq,bk))`, so added `j` values re-address an already-resident block. **The verifier confirmed the probe's "zero new work" claim AT THE INSTRUCTION LEVEL: `matmul_count` (2048), total bundles (16,624), spills/fills (16,653/22,820) and VMEM (18,948,096 B) are BYTE-IDENTICAL across pad_kv 0/1/4** — only a runtime grid-loop trip count changes. Measured marginal cost of a dead program **~0.14 us**, so deleting all 384 is worth **1.008-1.023x**. ABORT RULE FIRED; cand2-cand7 gate-closed; the compact grid, its scalar-prefetched live-pair table and its row-grouping variant were never written. **THE PREMISE WAS ARITHMETICALLY RIGHT AND ECONOMICALLY WRONG:** 37.5% of programs really are dead, and they are also nearly free, because v001 already guards them with a `pl.when` and pins their index maps so Pallas's revisiting optimization collapses their DMAs. **A COUNT OF THINGS YOU CAN DELETE IS NOT A MEASURE OF WHAT THEY COST** — the stub said so when refusing to set its bar from the 37.5% figure, and cand1 turned that caution into a number. **TWO PROBE-DESIGN LESSONS, BOTH EARNED HERE.** (1) **The FIRST probe design was CONFOUNDED and is kept, not deleted**: it padded k/v/bias with `jnp.concatenate` INSIDE the timed function, growing the (H,S,S) bias 2 GiB -> 4 GiB at pad_kv=4, and read 6.90/13.47/16.43/18.21 ms => ~3.7-6.4 us/dead-program and a **>25% ceiling, off by more than an order of magnitude**. **PARITY PASSED AT EVERY POINT, so no gate could see it**; only the magnitude being implausible for a no-op program gave it away. Verifier quantified the confound (clean 0->4 delta 0.42 ms vs confounded 6.57 ms — a **15.6x gap**) and confirmed the rewrite removed it. **Earned rule: when a probe measures a cost, check that NOTHING IN THE TIMED REGION SCALES WITH THE PROBE PARAMETER except the thing being priced.** (2) **The verifier then found a SECOND, subtler artifact in the CLEAN probe — in the author's favour — and the page adopted the correction**: `_v2_kernel` writes on `pl.when(j == n_kv-1)` but computes on `pl.when(j <= _last_live_kv(i))`, and at pad_kv=0 those CO-OCCUR for the last query row (i=3, n_kv-1 == last_live_kv(3) == 3); any pad_kv>=1 DECOUPLES them, a **binary** one-time event (+1.193 us/prog at 0->1, then a flat 0.11-0.18 us/prog plateau through pad_kv=20) that does NOT recur in the 384 real dead programs nor in a row-grouped compact grid. Folding it into a 4-unit average INFLATED the author's headline 1.023x; the de-confounded ceiling is **1.008x**, further under the bar. **AN AUTHOR'S "MOST FAVOURABLE TO THE LEVER" READING CAN STILL BE WRONG IN THE LEVER'S FAVOUR, and reporting it as the headline is how a sub-bar prize almost survives a bar.** Verifier extended the sweep to 8 points (adding pad_kv 1, 2, 16, 20 that the author never measured), reproduced every author point within 0.02-0.34%, and ruled: **"building the compact grid would have been a wasted candidate."** Base unchanged: 2.5395x author / 2.5375x verifier vs naive, parity PASS both seeds at bit-identical max_abs across all 12 probe points, firing CONFIRMED, 97.1% device-busy. **METHODOLOGICAL RESULT WORTH TRANSFERRING BEYOND THIS FAMILY: PRICE THE CEILING BEFORE BUILDING THE MECHANISM.** v002, v003 and v004 each built a mechanism and only THEN discovered its ceiling was too small; v005 spent one candidate (~15 min) and refuted without authoring anything. **Frontier unchanged at v001; four experiments, four refutes, nothing banked.** **NOT at ceiling, but the remaining space is now sharply characterized:** every lever touching PLACEMENT (v002), SIZING (v003), SCHEDULING (v004) or PROGRAM COUNT (v005) is measured and closed; what remains untried all changes **the arithmetic actually issued** — split-loop/unmasked-prefix, causal diagonal subtiling >= the 256 MXU edge, MXU-lane-aligned kv sub-blocking, heads-per-program, const-shift softmax. At 34.6% of the bf16 MXU envelope and 97.1% device-busy, the headroom that exists is on-device arithmetic, not overhead. Rank-1 => v006: split-loop / unmasked-prefix + causal diagonal subtiling, priced-first per v005's own validated methodology.

- **sparse-moe v001 opus5-process (2026-07-29)**: **supported at 2.4329x — but the registered mechanism ALONE missed its own bar, and the win came from a lever the stub called a "free implementation choice".** Hypothesis: the reference is sparse in name only (computes all `N=8` experts per token, then multiplies `N-K=6` of them by exactly `0.0` in the `bsne,bsn->bse` combine), so `N/K = 4.00x` of its 23.091 TFLOP is **algebraically** dead; evaluate only the `T*K=16384` selected (token,expert) pairs, expert-sorted, via grouped matmul. Bar 1.50x. **cand1 — the mechanism exactly as designed — returned 1.4462x and MISSED THE BAR.** A labeled wrong-math diagnostic (dense proxy, same shapes, one expert's weights) decomposed it decisively: routing+permutation+gather is **0.566 ms = 2%** (the indirection is NOT the cost), the dense ideal for the three contractions is **8.676 ms**, and the ragged down projection ran **14.815 vs 2.590 ms = 5.72x slower than dense** — one badly-tiled grouped matmul ate the entire 4x. **THE FINDING: `jax.lax.ragged_dot` lowers on TPU to a Mosaic `tpu_custom_call` carrying `frontend_attributes={ragged_dot_tiling="256,11776,256"}` — XLA picks the tile ITSELF, and it is STEERABLE FROM JAX via `jax.experimental.xla_metadata.set_xla_metadata(ragged_dot_tiling="m,k,n")`.** At XLA's tm=256 each ~2048-row group spans ~8 m-tiles and re-reads that group's 117 MB weight block per tile. Swept per projection: down `(1024,1024,4096)` and up/gate `(512,4096,2048)`. **`m` must divide `T*K`** (768/1536/640 rejected with `expecting m % mt == 0`). **THE DECLARED ESCALATION WAS A DEAD RUNG:** `megablox.gmm` matched `ragged_dot` to within **0.5% at every shared tile** — they are the same Mosaic kernel family, so the ENGINE was never the lever, the TILE was. **AND MOST OF THE TILE SPACE WAS BEHIND A FALSE WALL:** at the 32 MB scoped-vmem default, `(1024,1024,4096)` / `(2048,*,4096)` / `(512,1024,4096)` all died `CompileTimeScopedVmemOom`; `--xla_tpu_scoped_vmem_limit_kib=98304` took XLA's OWN untouched tile from **14.798 -> 3.528 ms (4.2x)** and the explicit tile added a further 6%. This is BRIEFS false-wall STEP-0 at full strength — the declared cand4 2x2 grid lay entirely inside the OOM region and could not have found the optimum (extension recorded). **FLAG-CLASS RESULT REPORTED SEPARATELY, and the stub's prediction for it was wrong by 3x in the direction that mattered** (predicted `<=1.05x`): scoped_vmem=98304 is **1.157x on the UNMODIFIED naive** (36.324 -> 31.382 ms), declared grid 49152 **0.986x** (a regression) / 65536 **1.025x** / 98304 **1.157x** — monotone to the top cell, so the optimum is NOT bracketed (v003 lead). **THIS IS A kernel-needs-flag CELL, not a flag-only cell** — the frontier candidate does not COMPILE at the 32 MB default (44.00M vs 32.00M scoped allocation), so the naive is co-measured WITH the flag and 2.4329x is the honest number; against the unflagged naive it is 2.822x but that framing mixes flag+kernel and is not claimed. **THE ADVERSARIAL VERIFIER PAID FOR ITSELF TWICE.** (1) **It found a semantics defect no gate could see: the candidate returned bf16 where `baseline.workload` returns FLOAT32** (the reference's `jax.nn.one_hot` is f32 and promotes its final einsum; `router_probs` is bf16 because softmax preserves dtype) — silently dropping ~8 output bits while parity PASSED, because the error is dominated by the bf16 GEMMs either way. Fixed by promoting the scale+accumulation to f32: **-0.7% p50, and mean_abs parity ratio IMPROVED 1.31 -> 1.16**. **Earned rule: diff the RETURNED DTYPE against the reference — a tolerance gate calibrated on GEMM noise cannot see an output-precision reduction.** (2) **It reproduced BRIEFS §6 COMPILE-CACHE HYGIENE *inside kgate's own verify path*:** its first `--mode screen` reported `hlo_firing_audit: FAIL`, `custom_call_count: 0`, "pattern not found in ENTRY HLO" — because kgate's shared `/tmp/kgate-verify-cache/<hash>` was warm, so no compile, so no dump, so the audit grepped a dump never written (last module `jit__normal`, never reaching `jit_workload`). `--fresh-cache` gave PASS / 17 custom calls at identical timing. **A FALSE FAIL is the benign direction, but the same blind spot would let a genuine silent no-op pass if the NAIVE leg were the cached one** — reported to the master, `tools/kernelgate` off-limits. The verifier also independently re-measured the pre-fix candidate at **2.4477x** (vs author 2.4490x, reproduction MATCHES 1.0011) and proved the permutation is an exact bijection with **every token receiving exactly K=2 contributions (min=max=2)** — no capacity-style token drop, which would have been `invalid`. It then **died on server-side API errors (500, then 529) before emitting its final report**, so its headroom-leads section never existed and its attack (d) never ran; (d) is instead covered by the frontier receipt's third parity seed. Recorded, not papered over. **NOT at ceiling: 448.5 useful TF/s = 48.9% of the 918 TF/s bf16 peak, and 77% of the same-shape dense ideal.** Op attribution: the three grouped contractions are **9.32 of 12.87 ms (72%)**, glue 2.50 ms. Rank-1 => **v002: fuse SwiGLU into the grouped matmul** so `g`/`u`/`h` (470 MB each, ~2.35 GB of round-trip) never reach HBM — with the class page's weight-re-read caveat (expert weights are 2.819 GB; one program per (row-block, expert) with the weight block resident is the required shape).
- **sparse-moe v002 opus5-process (2026-07-29)**: **refuted ON A PRICED CEILING WITH NO KERNEL AUTHORED — the flex-attention v005 methodology applied a second time and paying off a second time, and it kills v001's OWN rank-1 lead.** Hypothesis: collapse the gate->SwiGLU->down chain into one Pallas grouped-MoE kernel so `g`/`u`/`h` (2.82 GB of round-trip that three separate Mosaic `ragged_dot` custom calls are structurally forced to pay) never reach HBM; bar 1.06x over the v001 frontier, calibrated to a **1.172x** traffic ceiling (2.82 GB / a MEASURED 1.49 TB/s, itself derived from v001's own `silu(g)*u` fusion: 1.41 GB in 0.947 ms) and to the class page's **verified 1.136x** precedent for this exact full-fusion shape — deliberately NOT to the 4x of the original FLOP-elision story. **cand1 was a pricing probe with a pre-registered abort rule and it FIRED; cand2-cand5 were gate-closed and no kernel was written.** **THE BOUND, AND IT IS THE TRANSFERABLE RESULT: fusing this chain makes it IMPOSSIBLE to read the expert weights once.** Group boundaries are data-dependent (`[2117 2076 1984 2039 2069 2026 2013 2060]`) so they align to no static row-block extent; feeding the REAL `group_sizes` to `megablox.gmm`'s own `make_group_metadata` — the same function the kernel would have called — gives the EXACT `(row-block, expert)` program count the fused grid must run, and each such program reads its expert's full 3x117 MB weight set: **tm=512 -> 39 programs / 13.74 GB · tm=1024 -> 23 / 8.10 GB · tm=2048 -> 15 / 5.28 GB · tm=4096 -> 11 / 3.88 GB, versus 2.82 GB for the unfused three-matmul form. EVERY row is above it.** Fusion saves 2.82 GB and spends 1.06-10.92 GB; best case (tm=4096) nets 1.76 GB = 1.18 ms = a **1.10x** ceiling, every other tm nets NEGATIVE. **AND the tm that minimises weight traffic is measured catastrophic on the contraction itself:** sweeping tm ALONE (tk=1024, tn=4096, n=100) on the down projection gives 4.549 / **3.346** / 8.004 ms at tm=512/1024/2048 for `ragged_dot` and 4.566 / 3.412 / 9.630 for `megablox.gmm` — **tm=2048 costs +4.66 ms on ONE of three contractions against a fusion saving of at most 1.89 ms**, and tm=4096's `out_acc[4096,4096]` f32 accumulator alone is 64 MB, over half the chip's VMEM before any operand tile. **Structural statement of the bound: at this op-point each expert's weights are 352 MB and its own compute is 721 GFLOP ~= 0.786 ms at peak, so the weight read (0.236 ms) is ~30% of that expert's compute, with only ~2048 rows per expert to amortise it over. The three separate contractions are NOT a missed fusion opportunity — they are HOW the weights get read exactly once, and the g/u/h round-trip is the cheaper side of that trade.** **SCOPE DISCIPLINE, stated on the page:** what is refuted is full-fusion *via a (row-block,expert) grid* at this op-point; what is NOT refuted is that the round-trip is real (v001's cand5 — `preferred_element_type=f32`, -12% from doubling exactly this traffic — is direct family evidence it sits on the critical path), only that FUSION is the wrong instrument because its own weight cost is larger. **NO PARITY CLAIM IS MADE AND NONE IS AVAILABLE** — no candidate existed, so this refute explicitly does NOT rest on a parity-passing candidate that lost; it rests on a ceiling below the bar *before correctness enters*, which is the only legitimate kernel-free refute, and the page correspondingly does NOT claim at-ceiling and does NOT take the `refuted`-bound-confirmed exemption. **`verified_by` is set to an explicit n/a WITH ITS REASON rather than a stamp** — there is no kernel to re-measure, no parity to re-check, no custom_call to audit, and the firing audit is declared N/A BY CONSTRUCTION with its substitute named (the exact program count from the kernel's own metadata function, which proves what the mechanism would have COST before it was written, where a firing audit only proves one RAN). **Frontier untouched at 12.8728 ms / 2.4329x; nothing banked; family stays live at 48.9% of the bf16 MXU peak.** **PROCESS NOTE — a plan amendment made BEFORE any candidate ran, recorded with its reason rather than silently:** the original cand1 was going to price the *traffic* by materialization-ablation, and was re-specified on two grounds — (i) a Mosaic `tpu_custom_call` MUST materialize its output, so `g`/`u` cannot be ablated away and the remaining knob (output width) moves arithmetic and output bytes COLLINEARLY, so the ablation could not separate them and the derivation was already tighter; (ii) the quantity that actually gated the decision was the hand kernel's MXU efficiency, unbounded in the losing direction — and that risk was ALREADY RETIRED by v001's own cand4b, which showed `megablox.gmm` (itself a hand-written *Pallas* grouped matmul) matches Mosaic within 0.5%, i.e. a Pallas grouped matmul achieves the needed efficiency. Solving `fused ~= 6.289/eta + 1.95 ms` against the bar gave the real gate `eta >= 0.618` against a measured 0.65. **Rank-1 => v003: finer `tk`/`tn` granularity + layout variants on the RAGGED tile** — cand1's own tm curve is new evidence for it (a sharp interior optimum), and note `m` must divide `T*K = 16384` so tm admits only 1024/2048 in range and the finer axis is `tk` (multiples of 128), not `tm`. Leads 2-4 (in-kernel DMA gather 0.903 ms, scoped-vmem above 98304, the f32 scale+scatter combine 0.649 ms) all survive the abort intact — none required fusion.
- **sparse-moe v003 opus5-process (2026-07-29)**: **refuted — the hypothesis' PREMISE was confirmed and its ECONOMICS were not, and the result is a rare counter-example to a standing BRIEFS rule.** Hypothesis: `ragged_dot`'s tile optimum lies OFF the power-of-two grid v001 swept; a 128-multiple `tk` sweep (+`tn` refinement) yields >=1.02x over the 12.8728 ms frontier. **The tile response IS strongly sensitive — a 1.9x spread (3.2895 -> 6.2902 ms) across the swept `tk`, far exceeding the predicted >=5% — but v001's five-point coarse grid had already landed WITHIN 1.6% of the refined optimum**, so the reachable gain was an order of magnitude below the bar. Best point `tk=1792` (down projection): isolated leg 3.2895 vs anchor 3.3418 (1.0159x); whole-workload randomized-interleaved n=200/leg coin-flip-order A/B 12.7695 vs 12.8478 ms = **1.0061x, delta +0.0782 ms clearing 3-sigma pooled (0.0589) with bootstrap 95% CI [+0.0756,+0.0806] EXCLUDING ZERO** — i.e. **real, not noise** — but **1.0072x** against the banked frontier, under the 1.02x bar.
